  - the shared virtio queue path - `src/user/drivers/core/src/virtio.rs` - for DRV-001;
  - `src/user/services/manifest.toml` - one `dma-policy` row for this driver.

  THE THREE-PART CONTRACT, STATED SO IT IS TESTABLE (DRV-003 / WIRE-002). `serve_write` computes
  `bytes = count * SECTOR`, checks `span.fit(bytes)` - which bounds the DESTINATION DMA span - and
  then copies `bytes` OUT of the mapped source without ever asking how big the source is. Before the
  copy it must check, on the handle the caller transferred:
    TYPE     `object_type == OBJECT_TYPE_MEMORY_OBJECT`. `sys_memory_map` already refuses a wrong
               type, so this is a typed refusal to the caller rather than a fault;
    RIGHTS   `rights & RIGHT_READ`. The mapping path takes `Rights::MAP` and asks for nothing else,
               so a handle without `READ` maps today and is read out of - which is the half of the
               original finding that survived two narrowings;
    SIZE     `size >= bytes`. This is the whole of the copy defect: `count = 1000` against a
               512-byte object drives a 512 kB read out of a 512-byte mapping.
  All three are available from one `object_info` call, so the contract costs one syscall per write.

  DRV-002, THIS ITEM'S HALF: the block path silently clamps a count instead of refusing it and never
  validates the LBA against the device's capacity. A clamp turns a wrong request into a wrong WRITE,
  which is worse than a refusal. Both paths refuse out-of-range with a typed status. The USB
  32-bit-LBA truncation is `xHCI maintenance`'s half of the same ID.

  DRV-001: virtqueue size, used IDs, used lengths and used-index movement are not validated
  centrally, and a device - or anything acting as one - controls those fields. Validated once in the
  shared path so every virtio driver inherits it.

  EXCLUDED, so the item cannot grow: no new block operations, no `virtio-scsi`, no SCSI core, no
  block-wire extraction, no changes to the destination `Span` accounting, and nothing in
  `usb_storage` - the USB half of DRV-002 and DRV-003 belongs to `xHCI maintenance` and is gated
  there.

  FIXTURE AND ORACLE: QEMU's `virtio-blk-pci`, which the harness already configures on all three
  targets.

  AND THE ISOLATION GATE'S TRAFFIC PHASE IS NOT THAT ORACLE, WHICH THIS ROW ASSERTED WITHOUT READING
  IT (corrected 2026-09-04). The phase boots `-machine q35` with a `virtio-iommu-pci` and ONE
  endpoint, `virtio-net-pci`, and its medium is a SATA `-cdrom` read by firmware; its four assertions
  are that the controller translates, that an endpoint attached, that `driver.virtio-net` came
  online, and that a DHCP lease was obtained. There is no `virtio-blk` device on that command line
  and no volume-read assertion anywhere in the gate, so "a regression shows up as a boot that no
  longer reads its volume" described a check that does not exist - and this row could have been
  closed with its own driver never exercised under translation.

  SO THE ORACLE IS PART OF THE ITEM. The positive traffic phase gains a `virtio-blk-pci` endpoint
  behind the controller, carrying a run-private system-volume disk, with `iommu_platform=on` like
  the net endpoint beside it. Its guest assertion must identify that provider by its binding/BDF and
  read expected volume bytes THROUGH THAT PROVIDER. A generic mounted-volume line is insufficient:
  the shipping medium also embeds a volume, so fallback could mount successfully without the added
  driver serving a read. The gate checks the selected provider and the successful read as well as
  the existing DHCP result, and removing the block endpoint or breaking its read path must fail the
  block assertion. This is a positive-operation fixture extension; the dedicated `DMA_FIXTURE=1`
  hostile topology remains firmware SATA, IOMMU, EDU and virtio-net only. Until the block assertion
  exists this item's definition of done cannot be evaluated.

  MEASURABLE LIMITS: one `object_info` per write request; refusals are typed and allocate nothing;
  and the added validation must not move the P02M0162 bind window beyond its measured budget on any
  of the three targets, which is the inherited requirement the every-driver row states.

  DEFINITION OF DONE FOR ITS CLASS (driver): the driver binds and serves through the governed path
  unchanged; each of DRV-001, DRV-002's block half and DRV-003/WIRE-002's three parts has a HOST
  test that fails when the check is removed, named in the gate; the DMA policy is declared and
  admission reads the generated value; and the isolation gate's traffic phase still obtains a lease
  and reads the volume on x86_64, with the suite green on all three targets.

  ITS EVIDENCE HAS RUN (2026-09-14). `check.sh --gate qemu-virtio-iommu-x86_64` is green: the
  controller transitioned out of bypass, five hostile cases were refused by the hardware, a DHCP lease
  was obtained through the enforcing controller with real packets both ways, and THE SYSTEM VOLUME WAS
  READ THROUGH virtio-blk AT 00:04.0, attached to a domain behind the controller - which is this
  item's own oracle, named above. The host tests are `drivers::blk` (4) and the shared
  `drivers::virtio` (5), all passing, and the three-target suite is green: x86_64 394, aarch64 382,
  riscv64 385.
  AND THE GATE'S PRECONDITIONS ARE AN ORDER, NOT A STATE, which cost an afternoon to find and is
  written here so the next reader does not pay it again. The gate needs BOTH a shipping ISO keyed to
  this tree AND a `./test.sh` that accepts the build, and the volume has two shapes with two receipts:
  `built-x86_64-volume-test` for the shape the suite boots, `built-x86_64-volume` for the bootable one
  the ISO is keyed over. The order that satisfies both is
    ./build.sh --arch x86_64                                  # the test shape, and its receipt
    ./image.sh --format iso --dma-mode enforcing-required      # the bootable shape and the medium
    ./check.sh --gate qemu-virtio-iommu-x86_64
  Doing the first two the other way round leaves the test receipt stale and the gate fails INSIDE its
  own `./test.sh` with "the build does not match the sources", which reads like a build problem and is
  an ordering one.

- [x] **virtio-net maintenance.** Declare its DMA policy (it is the one device currently in the
  kernel's hard-coded list, so it is the migration's proof case) and gate that the generated registry
  value, not the list, is what admission reads.
  THE DECLARATION AND ITS GATE LANDED WITH THE REGISTRY FIELD (2026-09-09): `dma = "iommu-required"`
  in the manifest, the hard-coded list removed, admission reading the generated registry, and the
  DMA-mode gates refusing this row on a `no-iommu` boot by name and value.
  THE PARSER LAYER IS IMPLEMENTED AND TESTED 2026-09-12, awaiting its three-target suite run:

    REQUIRES   P02M0098 + P02M0161 + P02M0162, P02M0153's DMA/IOVA contract AND P02M0172's declared
                 policy - which landed - and the host-test seam
    OWNS       `drivers::net` - the link MTU, the slot arithmetic, where a received frame is and what
                 fits a transmit buffer; and `virtio_net.rs`'s use of them
    GATE       the three named host tests in `drivers::net`, plus the networking guest suites on all
                 three targets

  THE MTU IS THE DEVICE'S NUMBER AND IT SIZES EVERY BUFFER THIS DRIVER ALLOCATES. A zero was refused
  and everything else believed, so sixty-five thousand was half a megabyte of receive pool asked for
  on the device's word, and a link claiming to carry four bytes was a slot that cannot hold its own
  header. It is bounded between what IPv4 requires of a link and a jumbo frame, and anything else
  falls back to standard Ethernet.
  AND A USED ELEMENT IS AN INDEX AND A LENGTH. The shared ring check bounds the DESCRIPTOR; turning
  the pair into a pointer and a count is this driver's arithmetic, and a length past the slot it
  claims to be in reads into the next slot of the pool. All three ways it is not a frame - an index
  the pool does not have, a length that is the header and nothing else, and a length past the slot -
  are one tested decision.

  THE THREE-TARGET SUITE HAS RUN AND IS GREEN (2026-09-14): x86_64 394 tests, aarch64 382, riscv64
  385, all passing, over the images this tree builds. The host tests in `src/user/drivers/core` are
  48 and all pass. That is the evidence this item was waiting on.
- [x] **virtio-gpu maintenance.** Close DRV-010's display half against the current driver, declare
  its DMA policy, and prove the display endpoint's translated operation on the profiles P02M0173
  names. It was omitted from the first Phase-2 subset and its remaining work was riding on a future
  EDID item that nobody had started.
  THE DMA-POLICY CLAUSE LANDED WITH THE REGISTRY FIELD (2026-09-09): `dma = "trusted-untranslated"` in the
  manifest, read by admission from the generated registry.
  DRV-010's DISPLAY HALF IS IMPLEMENTED 2026-09-12. The host tests are `drivers::gpu` (8) and they
  pass; the three-target suite is green (2026-09-14: x86_64 394, aarch64 382, riscv64 385); and the
  x86_64 enforcing gate reports that "the display driver runs and a frame reached the screen" on the
  translated default machine.
  WHAT IS STILL OWED IS NOT A RUN BUT A PHASE (established 2026-09-14). This item's gate asks for the
  display endpoint under translation on the profiles `P02M0173` names, and those profiles DROP the
  display device by design: `check-qemu-iommu-ports.sh` boots a REDUCED machine because the full
  interactive one does not finish attach-and-map inside DeviceManager's boot window on an emulated
  port, and its own comment lists "the interactive display, input and audio devices, which no
  assertion here reads" among what it removes. So the proof this item wants cannot be obtained by
  running those gates as they stand; it needs a display phase added to them, with the boot-window
  cost that reduction exists to avoid measured rather than assumed. That is gate work with its own
  risk, it is named here rather than left as "awaiting a run", and it is what this box is waiting on:

    REQUIRES   P02M0098 + P02M0161 + P02M0162, P02M0153's DMA/IOVA contract AND P02M0172's declared
                 policy - this driver masters the bus - and the host-test seam
    OWNS       `drivers::gpu` - the display geometry contract; `virtio_gpu.rs`'s admission of the
                 device's reported size, its backing allocation, its rectangle union and its
                 transfer offset
    GATE       the five named host tests in `drivers::gpu`, each watched to fail under a mutation of
                 its check, plus the display endpoint under translation on P02M0173's profiles

  THE PHASE EXISTS NOW (2026-09-14), and it is the reduced machine PLUS EXACTLY ONE ENDPOINT.
  `check-qemu-iommu-ports.sh` gained a `display` phase on the two UEFI rows - `aarch64:uefi-gicv2`
  and `riscv64:uefi-aia` - each its own catalog key, its own log and its own census, registered in
  the model's row list and in the frozen release set. A direct `-kernel` boot is deliberately not one
  of them: it runs no loader, promotes no root, and never starts DisplayService, so there is nothing
  there to present a frame.
  WHY ONE ENDPOINT AND NOT THE INTERACTIVE SET: the reduction exists because the full machine puts
  about a dozen translated endpoints through attach-and-map inside DeviceManager's boot window and an
  emulated port does not finish that in time. `DMA_DISPLAY=1` is the reduced machine with the GPU
  added and nothing else, so the window the reduction protects is spent on the device the claim is
  about. AND THE COST IS MEASURED RATHER THAN ASSUMED: every phase of this gate now reports its own
  boot window, so the difference the added endpoint makes is a number in the log.
  THE ORACLE IS A FRAME AND NOT A DRIVER STATE. The driver reports online before any frame exists -
  it has a device, not a picture - so the phase requires the driver online exactly once, never
  restarted, and `ConsoleService: a frame reached the display`, with a failed present, a degraded
  admission and an untranslated bus master all refusals. That is the same oracle the x86_64 enforcing
  gate uses, on the interrupt path each port has.
  AND THE RUN IS DONE (2026-09-14). Both phases pass on the first run:

      aarch64:uefi-gicv2:display   the endpoint attached and translated, the driver ran without
                                     restarting, a frame reached the screen; 4 attachments,
                                     boot window 300s
      riscv64:uefi-aia:display     the same on AIA/IMSIC; 3 attachments, boot window 300s

  THE BOOT WINDOW IS THE NUMBER THIS CLAUSE WAS WAITING FOR, and it is the same 300 seconds the
  ordinary phases take: the reduced machine plus one endpoint finishes attach-and-map well inside
  DeviceManager's window, which is what the full interactive machine does not. The risk this item
  named - that adding the display back would cost the boot window the reduction exists to protect -
  is measured and is not there at one endpoint. Each phase kept its own census.

  THE DEVICE CHOOSES THE NUMBER THE DRIVER BELIEVES. Only a zero was refused, so four billion was
  accepted - and what an overflow does here is not a wrong picture: a wrapped `width * 4` describes a
  framebuffer whose rows are shorter than the pixels in them, and that description is handed to
  ConsoleService, which maps the buffer and draws into it. The geometry is now bounded by the image
  model's own maximum extent, with the pitch and the backing size both checked, and anything else
  falls back to the driver's own size.
  A WRAPPED UNION IS A RECTANGLE WHOSE CORNER IS BEFORE ITS ORIGIN. The rectangles arrive on a
  service channel, so they are input; `x + width` in a `u32` is where a crafted pair stops describing
  a shape. The union saturates, which gives a rectangle that is too large and which the clip removes,
  rather than one that is somewhere else.
  AND THE CLAMP BECAME A CLIP. Clamping an origin moves a rectangle that was entirely off the display
  to its edge, which presents pixels nobody asked to present; clipping keeps what was inside and
  presents nothing when that is nothing. The transfer offset - the last arithmetic before the device
  is told where to read - is checked, and a rectangle whose offset does not fit is refused rather
  than sent.
  THE MUTATIONS WERE WATCHED TO FAIL: believing the reported extent fails the geometry test, and
  wrapping the union fails the union test.

- [x] **virtio-input maintenance.** Declare its DMA policy and gate descriptor-parsing host tests
  over the new seam.
  THE DMA-POLICY CLAUSE LANDED WITH THE REGISTRY FIELD (2026-09-09): `dma = "trusted-untranslated"` in the
  manifest, read by admission from the generated registry.
  THE PARSING IS IMPLEMENTED AND TESTED 2026-09-12, awaiting its three-target suite run:

    REQUIRES   P02M0098 + P02M0161 + P02M0162, P02M0172's declared policy - which landed - and the
                 host-test seam
    OWNS       `drivers::input` - the axis range a device reports, the event fold and the coordinate
                 normalisation; and `virtio_input.rs`'s use of them
    GATE       the four named host tests in `drivers::input`, plus the input guest suites on all
                 three targets

  THE AXIS RANGE IS THE DEVICE'S CLAIM AND THE POINTER'S WHOLE COORDINATE SYSTEM. An `ABS_INFO` block
  shorter than the field does not contain it - reading it anyway returns whatever the config window
  holds - and a maximum that is zero, or negative once it is signed, makes the clamp an empty interval
  and puts the pointer in the corner whatever the device reports. Both are refusals with a fallback.
  AND THE EVENT FOLD IS FOUR RULES that had no test because they were four arms of a match inside an
  `unsafe` function over a raw address: an absolute axis sets, a relative one nudges and SATURATES
  rather than wrapping into the opposite corner, a wheel accumulates a momentary delta that saturates
  too, and a button is a bit that an unknown code does not touch. What is left in the driver is
  reading three fields out of the event the device wrote, which is the part that is `unsafe`.

  THE THREE-TARGET SUITE HAS RUN AND IS GREEN (2026-09-14): x86_64 394 tests, aarch64 382, riscv64
  385, all passing, over the images this tree builds. The host tests in `src/user/drivers/core` are
  48 and all pass. That is the evidence this item was waiting on.
- [x] **virtio-console maintenance.** Declare its DMA policy, and own the `console-bytes` provider's
  migration onto the catalogue - it is the one existing provider whose consumer is still special-
  cased, and the destination-migration table below names it for that reason.
  THE DMA-POLICY CLAUSE LANDED WITH THE REGISTRY FIELD (2026-09-09): `dma = "trusted-untranslated"` in the
  manifest, read by admission from the generated registry.
  THE MIGRATION IS IMPLEMENTED 2026-09-13, awaiting its three-target suite run before it is ticked.
  What it was, and what each piece is now:

    A VERSIONED DEVICE-SIDE CONTRACT, where there were three conventions. The wire was untyped
    messages in both directions: an EMPTY message meant the port would not take a write, a handle
    under a `BYTES` tag meant a replacement consumer, and the version was not on the wire at all.
    `liber:device@1` now carries `console-stream` - `attach` settles a version and a frame bound
    before a byte moves, `write` ANSWERS (`again` is a host that stopped reading, and it is the
    answer to the call that made the bytes rather than a message arriving out of order with it),
    and `receive` hands back a stream endpoint whose frames are `console-chunk`. A byte stream is
    the one wire whose disagreement is invisible - mismatched bytes are refused nowhere, they
    arrive somewhere else in the reader - so the refusal has to happen before the first byte.

    PROVIDER IDENTITY, where there was a driver's NAME. `route_offers` took the publication out of
    the catalogue, compared the binding's artifact name against `dev_channel`, started the agent on
    the channel it had taken, and CLOSED any other driver's publication of the same kind. The agent
    now holds `slot`, `provider-generation` and the binding's generation - the three the manager
    minted - so a withdrawal naming the publication that REPLACED the one in use is not mistaken
    for it, and a reused slot is not mistaken for its predecessor.

    SUBSCRIBE, ATTACH, DETACH, FAILOVER AND RECONNECT, where there was a private hand-off. The
    agent holds a `provider-catalogue` connection, subscribes to `console-bytes`, opens what it
    finds, and reattaches when a later publication arrives; a withdrawal of the one it is using
    ends the session it carried and leaves the subscription looking. A replacement agent does the
    same from scratch, so the manager no longer re-wires one: the `BYTES` tag and the driver-side
    `adopt` loop that waited for it are both gone, and a replacement's connection reaches the
    driver as an ordinary `CONNECT`. The driver reports `DISCONNECT` when its consumer goes, which
    is what frees the single-consumer slot the next `open` is checked against.

    AND THE MANIFEST STOPPED CLAIMING A PROVIDER NOBODY PUBLISHES. `virtio_console` declared
    `provides = [{ kind = "console-bytes", most = 1 }]` and publishes nothing at all - it brings the
    port up, writes its banner and stands. The declaration is removed rather than satisfied, and
    the reason is recorded in the manifest: a second `console-bytes` publisher would be
    indistinguishable from the development channel to a consumer that selects by kind, because both
    would speak the same version. Telling two publishers of one kind apart needs a ROLE the registry
    declares and the catalogue carries, and nothing in this tree has one. THE FIRST ITEM THAT ADDS A
    SECOND CONSOLE BYTE STREAM - `virtio-serial multiport` is the candidate - OWES THAT ROLE FIRST,
    and cannot close its integration gate without it.

    THE DECISIONS ARE SHARED AND HOST-TESTED. Version agreement, publication identity, the cutting
    of a frame longer than the bound, and what a refused write means are `driver_protocol::console`
    in the crate both ends already link, because a rule each end remembers separately is two rules.
    Seven host tests hold them, and each was watched to fail under a mutation of the rule it holds:
    identity by slot alone fails the withdrawal test, a partial write counted as a whole one fails
    the write-classification test, a version mismatch downgraded instead of refused fails the
    handshake test, and a span that stops one byte short fails the cutting test.

  THE THREE-TARGET SUITE HAS RUN AND IS GREEN (2026-09-14): x86_64 394 tests, aarch64 382, riscv64
  385, all passing, over the images this tree builds. The host tests in `src/user/drivers/core` are
  48 and all pass. That is the evidence this item was waiting on.
- [x] **virtio-snd maintenance.** Close DRV-009 against the current driver with its named negative
  test, and declare its DMA policy.
  THE DMA-POLICY CLAUSE LANDED WITH THE REGISTRY FIELD (2026-09-09): `dma = "trusted-untranslated"` in the
  manifest, read by admission from the generated registry.
  DRV-009 IS IMPLEMENTED 2026-09-12, awaiting its three-target suite run before it is ticked. The
  debt is four pieces of trust in one path and each is now a decision a host test holds:

    REQUIRES   P02M0098 + P02M0161 + P02M0162 (claim, handshake, rollback), P02M0172's declared DMA
                 policy - which landed - and the host-test seam, which this item's tests run over
    OWNS       `drivers::snd` - the period completion contract; `virtio_snd.rs`'s play and capture
                 paths and the reply they produce; and AudioService's reading of that reply
    GATE       the four named host tests in `drivers::snd`, each watched to fail under a mutation of
                 its check, plus the audio guest suite on all three targets

  ANY INTERRUPT WAS A COMPLETION. The MSI-X vector is shared with the queues this driver sets up and
  does not drive, so a wake with an empty used ring is ordinary - and the old path reaped whatever
  the ring held and called the period played. The wait is now bounded and repeats, and a period that
  never completed is a typed `NoCompletion` rather than a success.
  THE STATUS WORD WAS NEVER READ. The device writes `virtio_snd_pcm_status` into the last segment of
  the chain and it is where it says it could not play what it was given; every code that is not
  `S_OK` is now a failure, including ones this driver has no special handling for.
  A SHORT WRITE IS NOT A STATUS. The page is reused between periods, so the word beyond what the
  device wrote is the PREVIOUS period's status - an `S_OK` that belonged to a period that finished a
  moment ago. The status region is cleared before each submission and a completion shorter than the
  structure is refused before the status is believed. The capture side owes the PCM as well, and a
  capture that filled less than a period is refused rather than handed to a recorder as audio from a
  moment that has passed.
  AND THE ANSWER WAS ALWAYS SUCCESS, which is how the other three stayed invisible from outside the
  driver. A refused period is an EMPTY reply - the convention this driver already used for capture -
  and AudioService reads it: the stream stops being marked running so the next period re-prepares it,
  and a device that refuses eight in a row is treated as lost rather than fed forever.
  THE MUTATIONS WERE WATCHED TO FAIL: removing the status comparison fails two of the four tests, and
  believing a completion that is not there fails three.

  THE THREE-TARGET SUITE HAS RUN AND IS GREEN (2026-09-14): x86_64 394 tests, aarch64 382, riscv64
  385, all passing, over the images this tree builds. The host tests in `src/user/drivers/core` are
  48 and all pass. That is the evidence this item was waiting on.

  2026-09-02). This item listed DRV-003/WIRE-002 and then said generically that "every other High USB
  debt this file still lists closes in this item's gate" - which is the same prose-only correction
  the debt section warns about, one level down: an implementer following this bullet closes one ID
  and the other six are named nowhere they would be read. The debt mapping assigns SIX to this item
  and each is written out below with the assertion that closes it. Declare xHCI's DMA policy and
  record the class-module execution model the USB section below fixes, and close:
  (THE DMA-POLICY CLAUSE LANDED WITH THE REGISTRY FIELD, 2026-09-09: `dma = "trusted-untranslated"`
  in the manifest, read by admission from the generated registry. Every ID below is open.)

    DRV-002 (the USB half)     `usb_storage` clamps the transfer count silently and skips the LBA
                                 range check, AND truncates the LBA to 32 bits. Its negative: a read
                                 whose LBA is above 2^32 and one past the device's last block are
                                 both typed refusals, and neither is served from a truncated or
                                 clamped address
    DRV-003 / WIRE-002         `usb_storage::serve_write`, on the complete type/rights/size
                                 contract, alongside the virtio-blk half. Its negative: a request
                                 whose handle is the wrong object type, whose rights are short of
                                 what the operation needs, and whose declared size disagrees with
                                 the object are three separate typed refusals
    DRV-005                    flush converts every repeated failure into success without reading
                                 sense data. Its negative: a device that fails a flush twice is
                                 reported as a failed flush, with the sense read, and never as
                                 success
    DRV-006                    BOT transfer length and CSW residue are not validated. Its negative:
                                 a CSW whose residue exceeds the transfer, and one whose length
                                 disagrees with what was sent, are both refused rather than being
                                 read as a short success
    DRV-007, DRV-008           TWO DEFECTS AND THE GATE NAMED ONE (corrected 2026-09-02). The debt
                                 entry is "xHCI loses DMA handles and slot ownership across hotplug
                                 and partial failure, AND its synchronous waits consume and discard
                                 port-status events", and this row asserted only the second half - so
                                 the item could have closed with the resource half untouched. Both
                                 negatives:
                                   the EVENTS: a connect and a disconnect delivered in the same
                                     status window produce exactly one of each, and a storm of them
                                     is bounded rather than growing a queue;
                                   the RESOURCES: a device removed mid-enumeration, and an
                                     enumeration that fails part way, each leave no DMA handle
                                     allocated and no slot owned - counted before and after, and
                                     equal - and the same slot is usable by the next device to
                                     arrive at that port
    DRV-012                    descriptor transfers are unvalidated, and the debt names the SHORT
                                 record case this row had missed (corrected 2026-09-02): "USB
                                 configuration walkers read fixed fields outside descriptor records
                                 and accept short descriptor transfers". Reading past the end of a
                                 record that is too short to hold the field is a different fault
                                 from an overlong declared length, and only the second was gated.
                                 Its negatives: a descriptor whose declared length exceeds what was
                                 read, one whose type does not match what was requested, a
                                 configuration transfer SHORTER than the descriptor it claims to
                                 carry, and a walk over a record too short to hold the fixed field
                                 the walker reads - four typed refusals, and in the last two nothing
                                 outside the bytes actually received is ever read

  Any High USB debt this file lists that is NOT in that table is not covered by this item and needs
  its own named owner in the debt section before that item can close - which is the rule stated once
  rather than a sentence this bullet can satisfy by containing.

  ALL SIX ARE IMPLEMENTED 2026-09-12, awaiting the three-target suite run before this is ticked:

    REQUIRES   P02M0098 + P02M0161 + P02M0162, P02M0153's DMA/IOVA contract AND P02M0172's declared
                 policy - xHCI masters the bus - `rt::object_info`, and the host-test seam
    OWNS       `drivers::usb` (the Bulk-Only status wrapper and the flush decision),
                 `drivers::descriptor` (the configuration walk), `drivers::port` (what a port-status
                 change means), `blk::command_lba32`; and in the driver: `usb_storage`'s request
                 admission, write contract, transfer accounting and flush; `xhci`'s control-transfer
                 length reporting, resource ownership and port-change signal
    GATE       the named host tests in those four modules, each watched to fail under a mutation of
                 its check, plus the USB storage and HID guest suites on all three targets

  DRV-002's USB HALF. The count was CLAMPED into the transfer maximum and the LBA was never checked
  against the medium, so a request past the end of the disk went to the device as written and a
  request too large became a smaller one nobody asked for. Both are refusals now, through the same
  `blk::request_range` the virtio half uses. And the ten-byte SCSI command carries a THIRTY-TWO-BIT
  address: `lba as u32` on a request past two terabytes names a block near the start of the medium,
  so a write lands on somebody else's data and reports success. `blk::command_lba32` refuses it.
  DRV-003 / WIRE-002. `serve_write` mapped the transferred handle and copied `count * 512` bytes out
  of it without asking what it was, whether it could be read through, or how big it was - the same
  three questions the virtio half answers from one `object_info`, asked here from the same function.
  DRV-005. The flush turned every repeated failure into success without reading the sense data. A
  unit that does not implement `SYNCHRONIZE CACHE` has no volatile cache and the barrier holds without
  it - that is ONE refusal with ONE meaning, an illegal request naming an invalid operation code -
  and a unit that could not commit its cache is a FAILED barrier. The sense is read and decided on.
  DRV-006. The status wrapper has five fields and three were read: the RESIDUE and the transfer length
  were not, so a device that moved half a sector and said "pass" was a complete success and the rest
  of the buffer was the previous command's. The residue is checked before it is subtracted - one
  larger than the transfer would wrap into a "moved" of four billion - and a short transfer is a
  refusal for every caller but the sense read, which asks for it explicitly.
  DRV-012. The configuration walk trusted three things: that the transfer arrived (the page is reused,
  so the bytes past a short answer are the PREVIOUS descriptor's), that a record is as long as the
  field being read (an interface header whose `bLength` is two is legal, and the class byte at offset
  five of it is the next record's header), and that the answer is the descriptor that was asked for.
  All three are checked, in one walker both class drivers use, and the device-descriptor and
  hub-descriptor reads check their own lengths at their own call sites.
  DRV-007. Enumeration allocated a slot and three DMA pages and dropped their handles; a partial
  failure left the slot enabled and the pages pinned for ever, and a detach disabled the slot without
  closing anything. Every allocation is now owned by the device, `UsbDevice::release` is the ONE place
  that undoes what enumeration did, and it is called from the partial-failure path and from the detach
  path. A device the driver leaves addressed is held by the inventory rather than dropped, and the
  rings of a bound HID or storage device are released with it.
  DRV-008. The two synchronous waits took every event off the ring and dropped the ones they were not
  waiting for, so a device plugged in during a block read raised a port-status event into a wait that
  discarded it. The change is recorded where every event passes through, as ONE BIT - a storm cannot
  grow it - and the reconcile that follows reads each port's own register, which is what makes a
  connect and a disconnect in one window exactly one attach and one detach.
  THE MUTATIONS WERE WATCHED TO FAIL: ignoring the residue fails two tests, treating every flush
  failure as "no cache" fails one, truncating the address fails one, believing a short transfer fails
  one, and losing the pending port change fails two.

Each of these states its own three-line REQUIRES/OWNS/GATE triple before it starts, like every other
item in this index. None of them is a new device, none creates a shared contract that does not
already exist, and the debt IDs above are the specific already-shipping defects each one closes.

## 1. virtio devices

SEVEN OF THESE DEVICES RUN IN EVERY DEVELOPER RUN AND EVERY CI BOOT - blk, net, gpu, input, console,
snd and (through xHCI, which is group 2) USB - and the REST OF THIS GROUP DOES NOT (corrected
2026-08-30). The previous sentence said "this is the hardware this system actually runs on" of the
whole group, which is the same false premise the head already corrected for the Phase-2 subset and
which survived here. RNG, vsock, SCSI, crypto, balloon/mem and virtio-fs are not configured by the
harness at all, so for those the "cheapest to write and hardest to leave broken" argument does not
hold and they are ordinary phased backlog. virtio-iommu leads the ordering because it is the
isolation every other DMA driver here is measured against and its backend already exists.

**A DECLARED DMA POLICY PER DRIVER** - NOW OWNED BY [P02M0172](P02M0172.md), NOT BY THIS INDEX
  (2026-08-30). It was added here as a kernel/bus-infrastructure prerequisite when it had no owner;
  it has one now, and an index that keeps its own copy of an owned item is how two plans acquire two
  answers. What stays here is the DEPENDENCY: every DMA-capable item in all three groups is blocked
  on P02M0172, and the paragraph below is retained as the statement of what that milestone is for.
  The rule further down - every DMA driver follows an explicit `iommu-required` versus audited
  trusted-untranslated policy - had no mechanism, and a rule with no mechanism is a sentence.
  `system-manifest` has no such field, and kernel admission reads a hard-coded
  `IOMMU_REQUIRED_TYPES` containing exactly `VIRTIO_TYPE_NET`, defaulting every other device to
  `TrustedUntranslated`. So NVMe, AHCI, HDA, USB and every other DMA-capable driver added here runs
  untranslated by default, and the only way to protect one is to edit that list - the "source `match`
  arm" this milestone's own head forbids.
  P02M0172 owns: a REQUIRED policy field on every driver row in the trusted/generated registry, its
  validation, its propagation to exact-entry kernel claim admission, and refusal of a missing or
  unknown declaration - or of a bind whose declared isolation cannot be satisfied - rather than a
  silent default.
  AND A THIRD VALUE, `none`, WHICH TWO POLICIES CANNOT EXPRESS (2026-08-30). `iommu-required` and
  `trusted-untranslated` are both answers for a BUS-MASTERING driver, and this index also contains
  devices and helpers that never master the bus at all. Kernel claim admission enables bus mastering
  for every claimed PCI function, so labelling a non-DMA driver `trusted-untranslated` preserves
  authority it does not need and makes the registry unable to tell "audited DMA without translation"
  from "must never DMA". `none` is the declaration that it never does, and claim admission must
  honour it by leaving bus mastering OFF.
  Every DMA-capable item in all three groups is BLOCKED on it, because until it exists their declared
  policy cannot be expressed. **P02M0172 now owns and closes this prerequisite**, and this paragraph
  is an ORDINARY REFERENCE rather than a checkbox (corrected 2026-08-30): it was an unchecked item
  while its own text said it is not work in this index, so a reader and every counting tool saw an
  open task that can never close here. Nothing in this index ticks when P02M0172 lands; the items
  that depend on it stop being blocked.

**virtio-iommu** (a reference, not an item - see the DMA-policy paragraph above for why):
[P02M0153](P02M0153.md) owns the DMA/IOVA contract, its kernel-owned backend
  interface, its host fake and the IOMMU-absent policy - they MOVED there from P02M0098 - and it
  implements the backend and closes the first x86_64 EDU + virtio-net security slice. WHAT IS LEFT
  HERE IS A ROLLOUT AND IT IS NOT YET WORK: "the remaining drivers and architectures" is not a list,
  and an item without one cannot be picked up. It is therefore NOT a checkbox of this roadmap: the
  per-driver attachment belongs to the driver items that need it, expressed through the declared
  policy field above. P02M0173 owns the bounded emulated rollout of the existing virtio-IOMMU backend
  to the AArch64 and RISC-V QEMU profiles. The remaining NATIVE ARCHITECTURE work - VT-d, AMD-Vi,
  ARM SMMU and a native RISC-V IOMMU - is Phase 5's, one approved backend at a time. What survives
  here is the rollout's ordering statement and nothing that anybody closes - which is why this is no
  longer written as a checkbox. Device DMA remains denied until attachment succeeds; driver crash
  tears mappings down. Native backends remain separate Phase-5 work to the same contract.
  THE THREE-TARGET SUITE HAS RUN AND IS GREEN (2026-09-14): x86_64 394 tests, aarch64 382, riscv64
  385, all passing, over the images this tree builds. The host tests in `src/user/drivers/core` are
  48 and all pass. That is the evidence this item was waiting on.
- [x] **virtio-rng**: add a bounded entropy-source driver and a typed kernel/security
  ingestion contract that credits source quality conservatively, mixes rather than
  exposes raw host bytes, handles short/failing requests and never treats one VM source
  as proof of cryptographic health. Cover deterministic test injection, startup without
  entropy and driver restart without repeating previously delivered output.
  IMPLEMENTED 2026-09-13, awaiting its three-target suite run before it is ticked. What it is made
  of, and what each clause turned into:

    THE INGESTION CONTRACT IS `SYS_ENTROPY_ADD`, AND THE SUBMITTER DOES NOT SAY WHAT ITS BYTES ARE
    WORTH. The driver hands over bytes and its device capability; the KERNEL decides the credit. That
    division is the whole security of the call - a driver that could name its own credit could seed a
    machine to "fully seeded" with a constant and nothing downstream of `SYS_RANDOM_GET` could tell.
    The authority is the capability checked against the CURRENT claim on a function whose device type
    is the entropy device's, which is the rule `SYS_DEVICE_QUIESCED` already applies: a capability
    from a binding that is over is held by somebody who is no longer driving that device, and a claim
    on a NIC is not a licence to decide what this machine's randomness is made of.

    CREDITED CONSERVATIVELY, IN A CRATE A HOST TEST CAN REACH. `entropy::Pool` is outside the kernel
    for the reason `driver-binding` is. A paravirtual submission is credited at a QUARTER of its
    length and a hardware one at a half, no single submission is worth more than 512 bits however
    large it is, the pool holds at most 4096 bits so a device running for a month is not an
    arbitrarily well seeded machine, and below 256 credited bits `draw` answers NOTHING rather than
    answering weakly.

    MIXED, NEVER EXPOSED. The bytes go from the driver's DMA buffer into a SHA-256 chain and are
    never readable again: absorbing rekeys, drawing rekeys afterwards so a later reader cannot
    recompute what somebody is already using as a key, and the syscall clears its own scratch copy.
    Nothing in userspace ever reads a host byte, and the driver publishes no provider at all -
    there is nothing to connect to, which is the point.

    SHORT AND FAILING REQUESTS. A short answer is an answer and is submitted as it came; a request
    that times out leaves the descriptor with the device rather than reposting over it, because a
    reused descriptor corrupts a virtqueue for the life of the guest; an empty submission is credited
    zero AND STILL MOVES THE POOL, so a failing device cannot make the output repeatable.

    NEVER PROOF OF HEALTH. `SYS_ENTROPY_HEALTH` answers credit and provenance - how many bits, how
    many submissions, from which kinds of source, and whether this machine has a hardware instruction
    at all. The one boolean says the threshold was reached. Whether a machine seeded only by a
    paravirtual device is good enough for a given purpose is the caller's question, and a kernel that
    answered it would be answering for every future caller too.

    AND IT CLOSED A REAL HOLE. `SYS_RANDOM_GET` refused outright on a machine with no hardware random
    instruction, which is two of this system's three architectures - so anything wanting key material
    had nowhere to go and the only call that always answered was the one named `insecure`. A seeded
    pool answers instead.

    THE EVIDENCE. Eight host tests in `entropy` cover the refusal below threshold, the crediting rate
    and its ceiling, the per-submission bound, DETERMINISTIC INJECTION (the expected draw was
    computed by an independent SHA-256 written against Python's `hashlib`, so it asserts the
    construction rather than self-agreement), two draws never repeating, a RESTARTED DRIVER handing
    the same device bytes over again not repeating the output, and an empty submission still moving
    the pool. Two kernel tagged tests cover what only a running kernel can: a capability that names
    no binding is refused, the submission bounds are refused before the capability is even looked at,
    and a machine with no hardware instruction refuses the secure draw until the pool is seeded and
    answers afterwards. The harness attaches `virtio-rng-pci` on all three targets in test mode, so
    the guest suite boots with the device present; the profiles that do not attach one are what keep
    the unseeded refusal reachable.
  ITS EVIDENCE HAS RUN (2026-09-14). The eight `entropy` host tests pass, and the three-target guest
  suite is green with `virtio-rng-pci` attached in test mode: x86_64 394, aarch64 382, riscv64 385.
  AND THE RUN CORRECTED ONE OF THE TWO KERNEL TESTS RATHER THAN CONFIRMING IT. On aarch64 the secure
  draw ANSWERED where the assertion demanded a refusal, because the seeded pool this item added IS the
  second answer - and the test still said "a machine with no hardware source refuses", which was the
  contract before the pool existed. The disagreement was invisible on x86_64, where `RDRAND` takes the
  first branch. The assertion now states the three cases the syscall actually has: hardware answers,
  no hardware with a seeded pool answers from the pool, neither refuses with an untouched buffer.

- [x] **virtio-serial multiport**: generalize `virtio-console` from one debug console to
  dynamically named, hotpluggable ports with bounded control messages, per-port queues,
  open/close state and independent capability-scoped byte streams. Reserve the emergency
  console path, but let provisioning/diagnostic agents use separate ports instead of
  multiplexing an ad-hoc protocol over console bytes. Reuse P02M0104's specification-compliant
  static named development-port foundation; this task retains ownership of general
  dynamic discovery, hotplug and provisioning/diagnostic ports.

  ITS WRITTEN PLAN (2026-09-15), which the rule above requires before any code. Nothing here is
  started; what this is for is that the next implementer does not rediscover the three facts that
  decide the shape, and that a second item cannot make an incompatible decision about the contract
  they share.

    REQUIRES   the shared virtio queue path (`src/user/drivers/core/src/virtio.rs`), `common::bringup`
                 / `online_and_stand` and the DeviceManager claim they run under; the `ConsoleBytes`
                 provider kind and the catalogue's per-consumer `open`; the dev-channel framing in
                 `src/user/services/core/src/dev_agent.rs`; one `dma-policy` row, as every DMA driver
                 has
    OWNS       `VIRTIO_CONSOLE_F_MULTIPORT` and everything it brings: the CONTROL queue pair, the
                 control-message vocabulary, the per-port queue-index rule, per-port open/close state,
                 port NAMES, and one published provider per open port
    GATE       a new `check.sh --gate qemu-virtio-multiport` on a topology QEMU already supports and
                 this tree does not use: one `virtio-serial-pci` with a `virtconsole` AND a
                 `virtserialport,name=org.libersystem.dev` on the same bus, each on its own chardev
                 socket. THE OBSERVABLE EFFECT IS ON THE HOST SIDE OF THE GENERIC PORT: bytes the
                 guest writes to the NAMED port arrive on that port's socket and on no other, which
                 is a thing no single-port driver can do at all - and the console port's own socket
                 still carries the console. Plus host tests for the control protocol in
                 `user/drivers/core/src/port/tests.rs`' neighbourhood, each named in the verification
                 model in the same change.

  CLASSIFICATION: `driver` AND `DMA-capable`, so both prerequisite rows apply - the every-driver row
  and the DMA row - and the acceptance contract includes the DMA half: every buffer handed to the
  device is a live IOVA mapping in this binding's domain and the teardown returns them.

  THE THREE FACTS THAT DECIDE THE SHAPE, measured in this tree rather than assumed:
  1. THE HARNESS ALREADY PAYS FOR THE MISSING FEATURE. `qemu-run.sh` attaches the development channel
     as a `virtconsole` and says why in a comment: "without MULTIPORT there is no control queue to
     open a generic port with, so a `virtserialport` here never opens and the guest's writes go
     nowhere - measured, not assumed". The cost it names is a FIRMWARE PREAMBLE - UEFI writes its
     console output to every console-class device it enumerates - which the framing above the port
     skips today. A generic named port has no preamble, so this item retires that workaround.
  2. THE DESTINATION EXISTS AND IS NOT A NEW SERVICE. `ProviderKind::ConsoleBytes` is published by
     DeviceManager and subscribed by the development agent, so a port becomes a provider rather than
     a new IDL. What is MISSING is selection: `ProviderInfo` carries kind, address, generation, slot
     and liveness and NO NAME, so two open ports are two indistinguishable `ConsoleBytes` providers.
     Whoever writes this owns that field - a port name on the provider, or the ninth kind the
     head's rule offers - and the choice belongs in this plan's implementer's change, not before it.
  3. THE QUEUE INDEX RULE IS THE SPECIFICATION'S AND IS NOT A CHOICE. Port 0 keeps queues 0 and 1,
     the control pair is 2 and 3, and port `n > 0` takes `2n + 2` and `2n + 3`. A driver that
     numbered them any other way negotiates a device that answers nothing, which is the failure mode
     that looks like a dead port rather than a wrong index.

  ITS MEASURABLE LIMITS, because a control queue is an input: a bounded maximum port count with
  ports past it refused rather than allocated, a bounded control message length, a bounded per-port
  buffer count and size, and a port name length with a refusal past it. A device that announces
  four thousand ports gets the refusal at the first one past the cap.

  EXCLUDED, so the item cannot grow: no virtio-vsock, no change to the console PORT 0 path that
  ships today beyond the feature negotiation it needs, no new destination service, no dev-agent
  protocol change beyond selecting a named provider, and no hotplug of the PCI function itself -
  port hotplug through the control queue is this item's; device hotplug is the bus infrastructure's.

  WHAT HAS LANDED AGAINST THAT PLAN (2026-09-15), and what has not:
  - THE DECISIONS, as a module with fixtures. `drivers::console` holds the queue-index rule, the
    control message, the port state machine and the refusals, with seven host tests over the hostile
    half: a device announcing four thousand ports gets sixteen and a refusal BY NAME, a message
    shorter than its header is refused rather than read past, an event the specification does not
    define is refused rather than guessed, a name longer than the cap is truncated and kept, a repeat
    is one transition rather than two publications, and no port ever claims the control queue.
  - THE NEGOTIATION AND THE HANDSHAKE, in `virtio_console.rs`: `VIRTIO_CONSOLE_F_MULTIPORT` asked
    for and answered, the control pair set up, `DEVICE_READY` sent, every `PORT_ADD` answered with
    `PORT_READY` and every open answered with `PORT_OPEN` - all of it BOUNDED, because a device that
    stops answering must leave this driver online with the console it already has rather than
    spinning. A device that does not offer the feature is the single console it was, and which one
    this is is in the report.
  - THE TOPOLOGY THE GATE NEEDS, in `qemu-run.sh`: the test machine's `virtio-serial-pci` now carries
    a `virtconsole` AND a `virtserialport,name=org.libersystem.diag` on the same bus, each with its
    own capture file.
  - AND THE ORACLE READS: `driver.virtio-console: online (00:04.0, multiport 2/2/1, tx ok)` on the
    two-port device against `multiport 1/1/0` on the one-port one - announced over open over SERVED.
    A single-port driver cannot see the second port at all, which is what makes the count an
    observable effect rather than a liveness line. The third number is the one the control queue does
    not prove by itself: a port can be open at the device with nothing in the image able to reach it,
    which is exactly what this driver did when only the control half had landed.
  - THE NAME ON THE WIRE, which the plan named as this item's to own and which did not exist: the
    offer payload carries one now (`encode_offer_named`, bounded at forty-eight bytes, with a payload
    longer than the header read as a NAME rather than refused as a malformed frame), `ProviderInfo`
    has a `name` field, and DeviceManager carries it from the offer through the catalogue into every
    snapshot and every subscription frame. WHY IT HAD TO EXIST: one device publishing two providers
    of ONE KIND - a console port and a named diagnostic port are both `console-bytes` - is otherwise
    two entries a consumer cannot tell apart, because every other field is the same for both.
  - AND THE GATE IS NAMED AND GREEN: `./check.sh --gate virtio-multiport` boots the two-port machine,
    requires the driver's own `multiport 2/2`, and then reads the line the driver wrote to the
    GENERIC port off that port's own chardev capture - and requires its ABSENCE from the console's.
    That is routing rather than liveness: a single-port driver cannot see the port, open it or write
    to it. The console's own banner is asserted on the console capture in the same run, because a
    feature that cost the port that ships today would not be worth having.
  - AND THE PER-PORT BYTE PUMP, which was the last thing open. Every open GENERIC port is a
    `ConsoleBytes` provider now: a receive pool of eight four-kilobyte slots posted behind the port's
    own receive queue, a transmit buffer behind its own transmit queue, one consumer's session, and
    `liber:device@1`'s `console-stream` served over all of them from one loop - one thread, not one
    per port, because they share a device, an interrupt and a completion path.
  - IT IS THE DEVELOPMENT CHANNEL'S OWN CODE AND NOT A SECOND COPY OF IT. `drivers::serial_port` is
    the port, moved out of the dev-channel binary rather than duplicated: the constants, the bounded
    transmit wait, the typed refusals, the receive drain, the backpressure and the `Console` service.
    Two copies of a byte-stream contract is the one duplication whose divergence is invisible -
    mismatched bytes are refused nowhere, they arrive somewhere else in the reader - and the
    dev-channel driver had written down that a later multiport driver should carry the same protocol
    on a named port without anything above it changing. Both drivers now open a `serial_port::Stream`
    and run the same three calls over it.
  - THE CONSOLE PORT IS NOT ONE OF THEM, deliberately: port 0 carries the guest's console and the
    banner, a second writer on it would interleave with both, and a consumer reading it would be
    reading the boot log. What is published is the generic ports the control queue opened, up to the
    bring-up protocol's four publications per handshake - and the report says how many of the open
    ones that was, so a port open and unserved is visible rather than inferred.
  - AND THE CONSUMER SELECTS BY NAME. The development image publishes `console-bytes` from two
    places now, and the registry entry for this driver had been emptied with the reason written into
    it: "telling two publishers of one kind apart needs a ROLE the registry declares and the
    catalogue carries, and nothing in this tree has one yet". The name is that role: each port is
    published under the name the host gave it, the development channel publishes under
    `org.libersystem.dev` from a constant both ends read, and the agent matches on it - exact bytes,
    because a prefix rule would attach it to `org.libersystem.dev2` and a case-insensitive one to a
    port some host named in capitals, both silently. The handshake still confirms the contract: a
    name is a label the publisher chose and proves nothing about what is on the other end.
  - WHAT THE GATE PROVES NOW, beyond the routing it already did: the driver's SERVED count, and that
    the publication reached the catalogue as a `console-bytes` provider - `DeviceManager` counts two
    more kinds in its published-providers line, which had listed six of the eight and therefore read
    as "nothing published" for the other two.
  - AND THE WHOLE x86_64 GUEST SUITE PASSES WITH IT: 399 tests, with the console driver now taking an
    MSI-X vector and running a service loop where it used to stand on its bootstrap channel. That
    matters more than it sounds: the driver that carries this machine's console output changed from
    something that only answered pings to something that serves consumers, and the boot chain it sits
    in is the one every other test runs on top of.
  - AND SIX GATES THAT COULD NOT RUN AT ALL WERE REPAIRED, because they are the gates for exactly
    this code. `check-provider-catalogue`, `check-driver-connections`, `check-no-fixed-provider-slots`
    and `check-audio-provider-recovery` extract production functions by signature and compile them
    against host doubles; the signatures had lost an `unsafe` the runtime no longer needs, a mutation
    locator had lost a level of indentation - so a defect the gate claims to reject was not being
    injected at all - and one double was missing a field the extracted code reads. `development-build`
    demanded that a `cargo build` produce two programs that are linked from a pinned upstream and
    staged rather than compiled. `development-gate` declared ITSELF broken, three times over: it
    passed a 153 kB manifest through an environment variable, which Linux refuses past 128 kB per
    string; it resolved its own path after moving away from it, so every re-exec named nothing; and
    its `required-features` check read four lines past a program's name, which two lines of comment
    were enough to push the attribute out of. Each of the six failed as a traceback, a compile error
    about somebody else's function, or a message about itself - which reads like a broken tool rather
    than like an unverified claim, and is why they had stayed broken. All six are green, one with a
    new fixture for the name surviving publication into `provider-info`.

  AND THIS ITEM ANSWERED THE SERIAL-CONTRACT QUESTION, because it is the first of the three claimants
  to be implemented and the rule three sections down says the first one answers it for all three:
  "whether a virtio port, a UART and a USB adapter are one contract, or whether the byte stream is
  the base and the line-coding and control lines are a serial layer on top".

      THE BYTE STREAM IS THE BASE. `liber:device@1`'s `console-stream` is it, and it is deliberately
      not a serial port: `attach` settles a version, `write` answers with what it took, `receive`
      hands back a stream endpoint, and there is no baud rate, no parity, no stop bits, no DTR, no
      RTS, no break and no line-status event anywhere in it. `ProviderKind::ConsoleBytes` is what a
      publisher of one publishes, and a publication carries a NAME so several of them on one device
      can be told apart.

      WHY THE BASE IS NOT THE SERIAL PORT, and this is the half that had to be decided rather than
      drifted into. A virtio-serial port has NO line coding at all - the specification does not
      define one - so a common contract carrying baud and parity would make every virtio port answer
      a question it has no answer to, and the honest answer would have to be a refusal on every
      operation the base defines. A UART and a USB CDC-ACM adapter do have those controls, and both
      have them as a DIFFERENT set: CDC-ACM carries line coding in a class request and a UART in
      device registers. So the line-coding and control lines are a SERIAL LAYER, a second interface
      a driver that has them serves IN ADDITION to the byte stream - not fields on the byte stream
      that two of the three publishers would leave empty.

      WHAT A LATER CLAIMANT THEREFORE OWES, so this is a decision rather than an opinion. A 16550, a
      PL011 or a CDC-ACM adapter publishes `ConsoleBytes` and serves `console-stream` over
      `drivers::serial_port` - the module this item extracted for exactly that reuse, which is
      transport-free: it takes a receive queue, a transmit queue and a DMA buffer, and knows nothing
      about virtio beyond them. The serial layer is a SEPARATE interface with its own provider kind,
      owned by the first of those three that needs it, and a consumer that only wants bytes never
      has to hold it.

  WHAT IS DEFERRED, and it is not part of this item: the development channel still has a device of
  its own. Now that a named multiport port carries the same contract, the second `virtio-console`
  function and the firmware preamble it was accepted to avoid could both go - the port would be named
  `org.libersystem.dev` on the machine's one virtio-serial bus and the agent would find it by the
  same name it already asks for. That is a harness change with its own gates and belongs to whoever
  retires the workaround, not here.
**THE THREE-TARGET DRIVER RUN IS DONE (2026-09-18).** `./test.sh --tags drivers,storage,usb,network,audio`
passes on all three ports: **83 on x86_64, 80 on aarch64, 82 on riscv64**. The counts differ because
some oracles belong to one port - there is no test whose result differs between them. The two emulated
runs took fifty-two and fifty-seven minutes under TCG against three on x86_64 under KVM, which is the
factor that has kept them to the end of the milestone rather than inside its loop.

**AND IT FOUND A HARNESS DEFECT ON ITS FIRST ATTEMPT, recorded here because every driver item below
closes on that run.** `qemu_attach_suite_devices` attaches the NVMe controllers and
the vsock device only for a run whose tags include `slow` or which passes no tags at all. That
condition is a PROXY for "does this run assert on these devices", and it was wrong for one test:
`device_table_resources_the_nvme_controller` is `[Drivers, Pci]` with no `Slow`, so `--tags drivers`
selected it and did not bring its controller. It failed with "0 found" on a machine that had none.

IT PASSES ON x86_64 UNDER THE SAME TAGS, which is why nothing had caught it: that profile attaches
NVMe unconditionally, so the proxy is never consulted there. A defect visible only on the ports
nobody had run is the thing a three-target run exists for, and this is the first one it produced.

The condition now names the tags those tests actually carry - `slow`, `drivers` and `pci` - rather
than standing in for them.

- [x] **virtio-vsock**: implement bounded host/guest stream connections with negotiated
  CID, port capability, credit accounting, reset/shutdown and connection limits. Expose
  it as an explicit local transport for provisioning, diagnostics and agents, not as an
  ambient bypass around NetworkService, PermissionManager or authenticated remote-admin
  policy. Test hostile credit updates, peer reset and host disappearance.

  THE PLAN, AND THE ONE DECISION IT TURNS ON. vsock is a stream between this guest and its HOST,
  addressed by a context id and a port. Everything else about it follows from what kind of provider
  it is, and that is the decision: IT IS `local-stream` AND NOT `net`. A consumer of `net` is handed
  a link it can route and a stack above it; a consumer of this is handed connections to ONE peer, by
  port, with no addressing and no routing at all. Published as `net`, a host channel would become an
  ambient path around NetworkService reachable by everything that already asks for a link - which is
  exactly what this item forbids - and no amount of care inside the driver would take that back. So
  `local-stream` is a new kind in `liber:device@1`, in the manifest vocabulary and in the wire, added
  in the same change; the driver's manifest entry names it, and the oracle ASSERTS the kind rather
  than trusting the manifest, so a later simplification of two kinds into one fails a test.

  CREDIT IS WHERE A VSOCK DRIVER IS WRONG, silently and in both directions, so it is the part that is
  pure and host-tested. The window is computed from two free-running `u32` counters that the PEER
  moves, and the arithmetic has to be MODULAR: a subtraction that is not wrapping gives four
  gibibytes of window at the wrap and zero the other way round - the first writes into a buffer that
  does not exist, the second stalls a healthy connection for ever. And the peer's numbers are a
  claim: one saying it has taken more than it was ever sent is describing a window wider than its own
  buffer, which is the hostile credit update this item names, and it is REFUSED rather than trusted.

  THE THREE HOSTILE CASES THE ITEM NAMES, and where each one is answered:
    - HOSTILE CREDIT UPDATES: `may_send` answers `None` for a peer whose numbers do not admit a
      window, and the driver resets the connection rather than writing into it. A peer that sends
      PAST the window this driver advertised is the same defect from the other side, and the answer
      is the same reset - dropping the excess silently would leave the two ends believing different
      things about one byte stream for ever.
    - PEER RESET: a reset closes from EVERY state, including from `Connecting`, which is how a
      refused connection is told from a slow one.
    - HOST DISAPPEARANCE: every wait has a deadline in the 100 Hz ticks READY is measured in, and a
      connect that times out is a different answer from one that was reset. A wait with no deadline
      would make a host going away the driver's last act.

  DEFINITION OF DONE FOR ITS CLASS: host tests over the packet header, the credit window and the
  connection state machine against a peer that lies; the QEMU model proving the governed process, the
  DeviceManager binding and the destination contract; the bind window recorded; watched-fail
  mutations; registered in the verification model in the same change. Acceptance on aarch64 and
  riscv64 is a separate measurement on each and this plan does not claim it.

  **WHERE IT STANDS (2026-09-17): UP ON x86_64, AND ITS ORACLE IS THE ONLY ONE IN THE SUITE THAT
  LEAVES THE MACHINE.** `driver.virtio-vsock: online (00:0e.0, guest cid N)`. Every other device this
  tree drives is a MODEL: the bytes a driver writes are answered by code inside QEMU. vsock's other
  end is a process on the host, so the harness starts one - `src/harness/vsock-echo.py` - and the
  oracle sends a 256-byte position-dependent pattern, reads it back, and requires it to be identical.
  What came back has crossed the device twice and been through the host kernel's vsock stack and a
  program that read it.

  THE PORT IS THE GUEST'S OWN CONTEXT ID, which is not a trick but the only thing that makes two runs
  on one machine safe. A host vsock port is global to the host - one listener per port, whoever binds
  it first - while `/dev/vhost-vsock` already refuses a context id another guest holds. So the id is
  the unique thing and the port follows it. That is also why `OP_IDENTITY` is part of the wire rather
  than a diagnostic: a local stream's address is NEGOTIATED, the host chooses it, and a program that
  has to be reachable cannot learn it any other way.

  WHAT THE ORACLE ASSERTS BEYOND THE ECHO: that the driver publishes `local-stream`; that the context
  id it reports is above the three reserved ones, which a driver reading the wrong config offset
  would fail; that the whole of a 256-byte write is accepted, which is a statement about the credit
  window being computed the right way round; and that A PORT NOTHING LISTENS ON IS REFUSED RATHER
  THAN HUNG - the host answers a closed port with a reset, and a driver that ignored it would still
  answer, but only after its connect budget ran out and with a different status.

  THE BIND WINDOW IS 48 TICKS on x86_64, measured from the boot the suite runs, against the 200 the
  bind window allows - beside `xhci` at 40, `nvme` at 32 and `hda` at 23. Nothing in this driver waits
  on the host during bring-up, which is why it is in the same band as the others: the connect deadline
  is spent by a CONSUMER after READY, not before it.

  AND THE MUTATION GATE FOUND A HOLE IN ONE OF MY OWN TESTS, which is the return on running it rather
  than writing it. `a_send_claiming_more_than_it_carries_is_refused` used a declared length of 4096
  against a wire whose ceiling is 4088 - so it was refused by the CEILING, and the length-against-
  what-arrived check it is named for could be deleted with the test still passing. The gate said so in
  one line. The test now declares 64 bytes and carries four, and asserts that the claim is inside the
  bound so the hole cannot come back.

  **AND THE TWO THINGS THAT WERE OPEN ARE DONE (2026-09-19): THERE IS A TABLE, AND THE EVENT QUEUE
  IS READ.**

  CONNECTION LIMITS ARE A TABLE NOW AND NOT A BOUND OF ONE. Four streams, each with its own
  connection, its own credit and its own staging buffer, and the registry declares four publications
  of one consumer each - `consumers` stays ONE for the reason the entry always gave, that two
  consumers sharing a stream would interleave their bytes with nothing to separate them again. What
  four buys is four INDEPENDENT streams.

  THE STREAM IS FOUND BY THE PORT AND THE SLOT IS FOUND BY THE ENDPOINT, and those are two different
  questions that a single index would have answered wrongly:
  - AN ARRIVING PACKET NAMES ITS STREAM BY THE LOCAL PORT. One ring carries every stream, interleaved
    in whatever order the host sent them, and both ends of all of them share a context id and a host
    - so the port this driver assigned is the only field that tells them apart. A stream's port is
    `base + slot` by construction, so routing is arithmetic (`vsock::slot_of`), and a port OUTSIDE
    the range belongs to nobody: masking it in, which is what indexing by a remainder would do,
    applies a stranger's bytes to whichever stream the remainder lands on.
  - A CONSUMER'S STREAM IS FOUND BY ITS ENDPOINT VALUE AND NEVER BY ITS INDEX IN `Serving`, because
    `close_at` fills the hole it makes with the LAST entry - so an index names a different consumer
    the moment any other one leaves, and a stream found that way is somebody else's bytes under the
    right name.

  AND A RECONNECT IS NOT A SECOND STREAM. Asking to connect while this consumer's own stream is OPEN
  is a mistake - the wire has no way to say which of the two a later SEND is for - but asking after
  it has CLOSED is ordinary, and reuses the slot already held with an EMPTY staging buffer: bytes
  left from the connection that closed belong to a stream that no longer exists.

  THE EVENT QUEUE CARRIES ONE THING AND IT IS NOT ABOUT A CONNECTION. A transport reset says every
  connection is gone - after a migration, or after the host's device was reconfigured - which is a
  statement no connection's own state machine can make for itself. It is read before a consumer's
  request is answered, because that is the only moment its answer differs: the operation about to run
  is the one that would otherwise write into a dead connection and report success. An event this
  driver does not recognise is re-posted and NOT acted on, because treating an unknown number as a
  reset would close every connection on a message the specification may give a meaning to later. And
  a machine whose event queue cannot be set up or given a buffer serves its streams exactly as before
  and SAYS so, rather than refusing to bind over a queue almost nothing ever writes to.

  THE ORACLE SENDS BOTH BEFORE IT READS EITHER, deliberately: send-read-send-read would pass a driver
  with one connection's worth of state, because there would never be two streams' bytes in it at
  once. Two consumers, two DIFFERENT patterns, and each must read back its own. The host's own log is
  the other half of the evidence - `vsock-echo` records connections from ports 1024 AND 1025, which
  is the table handing out distinct local ports rather than one driver reusing a single one.
  MUTATION: routing every packet to slot zero fails it at the second consumer's connect with
  `STATUS_ERR` against `STATUS_OK` - that stream's response landed on the first stream, so it never
  left `Connecting` and timed out.

  AND THE THREE-TARGET RUN THIS ITEM RECORDS BELOW IS NOW OLDER THAN THE DRIVER. It was run against
  a single-connection vsock; the ports have to be measured again, with everything else, at the end.

  AND THE THREE-TARGET RUN HAPPENED (2026-09-18): the driver suites pass on all three ports - 83 on
  x86_64, 80 on aarch64, 82 on riscv64, the counts differing because some oracles are one port's - so
  "x86_64 only" no longer applies to this driver.

  AND IT WAS MEASURED AGAIN WITH THE TABLE AND THE EVENT QUEUE IN IT (2026-09-19), WHICH IS WHAT THE
  PARAGRAPH ABOVE SAID IT OWED. The driver suites pass on aarch64 (100 tests, 1539 s) and riscv64
  (102 tests, 2097 s) under `drivers,pci,slow`, each reporting `driver.virtio-vsock: online (..., guest
  cid N)` and each running the two-consumer oracle whose far end is a process on the HOST. THIS ITEM
  IS CLOSED: its definition of done asked for host tests over the header, the credit window and the
  state machine against a peer that lies, the QEMU model, the bind window, watched-fail mutations,
  registration in the verification model, and a separate measurement on each port. All of them are in.
- [x] **virtio-scsi**: negotiate transport features, enumerate bounded targets/LUNs,
  support read/write/flush and hotplug behind the canonical block contract, and cap
  queues, CDB/sense lengths and outstanding tags. IT OWNS THE TWO SHARED CONTRACTS ONLY IF IT IS THE
  FIRST OF THEIR CONSUMERS TO BE IMPLEMENTED, which is the rule this file states everywhere else and
  contradicted here (corrected 2026-08-30): UAS is independently ordered and free to be written
  first, and a fixed owner contradicted by a non-gating order is not an owner. Whichever of them is
  written first builds the contracts below; the others consume them. They are TWO contracts with
  different consumers, and an earlier draft ran them together. There is no common SCSI command/sense layer (SCSI lives inside
  `usb_storage.rs`) and no canonical block wire (copied by hand into three places). The
  SCSI core is needed by this item, by UAS, and by the USB BOT code it is EXTRACTED FROM -
  which is the third consumer and the one an extraction creates: BOT moves onto the common
  core in the same change, or the extraction has produced a second copy rather than a
  shared one. WHICHEVER OF THE THREE IS IMPLEMENTED FIRST OWNS THE CORE, per the
  first-implemented-consumer rule in the head; this item does not own it by being listed
  first. NVMe has its own command set and AHCI speaks ATA, so neither needs it.

  THE BLOCK WIRE HAS A RULE RATHER THAN A LIST, because a list of three was already wrong:
  UAS publishes block, so can SDHCI, and so can NFIT. It is owned by THE FIRST IMPLEMENTED
  SLICE THAT PUBLISHES A BLOCK PROVIDER, whichever that turns out to be. Naming
  `virtio-scsi` as the owner of both contracts made NVMe and AHCI wait on a SCSI layer
  neither of them uses; naming three candidates for the block wire then left out the
  others in the same breath.
  It complements rather than replaces `virtio-blk`; unsupported passthrough commands are
  not exposed to ordinary storage clients.

  **ITEM PLAN (2026-09-17), written before any code because the rule below says so.**

  CLASSIFICATION: `driver`, plus ONE shared library it owns by being the first of its three consumers
  to be written: the SCSI command and sense core. The block wire is already extracted and this item
  consumes it, so of the two contracts this bullet names, one is done and one is this item's.

  WHAT IT REQUIRES THAT ALREADY EXISTS. The virtio transport, which is why this is group 1 work: no
  new resource profile, no new discovery, no MSI-X to resolve - `common::bringup_features` and
  `virtio::Queue` are what `virtio-blk` already runs on. `driver_protocol::block` as the wire and
  `drivers::blk` for the request refusals. The bind window is reported for every driver by
  DeviceManager, so this item inherits that rather than arranging it.

  WHAT IT OWNS THAT DOES NOT YET EXIST: THE SCSI CORE, and the rule for it is already written above -
  whichever of virtio-scsi, UAS and the USB BOT code is implemented first owns it, and the BOT code
  MOVES ONTO IT IN THE SAME CHANGE or the extraction has produced a second copy rather than a shared
  one. That is not a stylistic preference here; the block wire had three hand-written copies and two
  of its servers had drifted into answering a refusal differently, which is what extracting it found.
  The core is command descriptor blocks, sense data and the capacity answer, as pure decisions with a
  host test each:
    - THE TEN-BYTE COMMAND CARRIES A THIRTY-TWO-BIT BLOCK ADDRESS, which `drivers::blk` already
      refuses to truncate and which the core must build correctly for the addresses it does admit.
    - SENSE IS A KEY, AN ADDITIONAL CODE AND A QUALIFIER, and the three together are what says
      whether a failure is retryable, a medium change, or a unit that is simply not ready yet. A
      driver that reads only the key retries a permanent failure for ever and gives up on a unit that
      was about to become ready.
    - THE CAPACITY ANSWER IS THE LAST BLOCK AND NOT THE COUNT, which is the off-by-one that makes a
      driver read one block past the end of every medium it serves.

  WHAT IT EXCLUDES: passthrough of commands this core does not model, which the item text requires be
  kept from ordinary storage clients; hotplug beyond enumerating what is present at bring-up, because
  this tree has no arrival event; the event queue beyond bounding it; multiple targets and LUNs
  beyond the first that answers, deliberately, so the first slice has one provider like every other
  storage driver here; and sixteen-byte commands, which are what a medium past two terabytes would
  need and which `drivers::blk::command_lba32` refuses rather than truncates.

  TARGET DEVICE MODEL: QEMU `-device virtio-scsi-pci` with a `scsi-hd` behind it.

  OBSERVABLE-EFFECT ORACLE, to the same convention as the four before it: a capacity answered from
  READ CAPACITY rather than from anything the test said, a pattern written to a block and read back
  and COMPARED, and a range past the last block refused with the typed status rather than clamped.

  MEASURABLE LIMITS: bounded request and response queues; the CDB length fixed at ten bytes and the
  sense buffer bounded, both refused rather than truncated past their bounds; one request outstanding
  on the first slice; a bounded wait with a typed timeout.

  DEFINITION OF DONE FOR ITS CLASS: host tests over the CDB building, the sense decode and the
  capacity parse with malformed answers, and a mutation for each in `driver-mutations`; the USB BOT
  path moved onto the same core in the same change, with the whole suite proving it still serves its
  stick; the QEMU model proving the governed process, the DeviceManager binding and the block
  contract; the bind window recorded per target. Acceptance on aarch64 and riscv64 is a separate
  measurement on each and this plan does not claim it.

  **WHERE IT STANDS (2026-09-17): THE SHARED CONTRACT IS DONE AND THE DRIVER IS HALF DONE.**

  THE SCSI CORE IS THE PART THAT LASTS, and it is finished. `drivers::scsi` is the command descriptor
  blocks, the sense decode and the capacity parse as pure decisions, with twelve host tests and three
  mutations. THE USB STORAGE PATH MOVED ONTO IT IN THE SAME CHANGE, which is the rule this file states
  and the whole reason the extraction was worth doing now: six opcodes and two hand-built command
  blocks left `usb_storage.rs`, and the suite still reports `driver.xhci: online ... (storage)`, so the
  stick is still enumerated and served through the shared core. An extraction that left the original
  behind would have produced a second copy, which is what the block wire's three divergent copies cost
  before they were merged.
  WHAT THE CORE CAPTURES ARE THE THREE SILENT MISTAKES: a block address written little-endian, which
  names a plausible address on the same medium that nothing refuses; a capacity read as a count when
  READ CAPACITY answers with the LAST BLOCK, which makes a driver read one block past the end of every
  medium and surfaces as an I/O error on the last sector rather than as anything naming the
