  speaking one command set over different pipes, and the catalogue's token is what tells them apart -
  the thing a token has always been for and which nothing had exercised. The oracle asks for the
  SECOND block provider by position rather than by kind, which is the shape a consumer needs.

  **THREE THINGS WENT WRONG AND EACH IS WORTH THE LINE**, because two of them are about this tree
  rather than about UAS:
    - THE LUN FIELD IS A SCSI SINGLE-LEVEL LUN, EIGHT BYTES OF ZERO for unit zero, and the first
      version put `scsi::virtio_lun` there - virtio-scsi's four-byte addressing form with a leading
      one. That addresses a unit which is not there, and the device answered nothing at all. A shared
      command core is worth having and it does not make every field shared: the LUN belongs to the
      TRANSPORT.
    - THE BRING-UP RETRIED SIXTEEN TIMES WITH A SPIN BUDGET EACH, which is sixteen budgets - and on a
      device that answers nothing that is a driver which never reports at all. This tree has paid for
      that once already, in the audio driver, and the rule it wrote down is the rule: one deadline in
      TICKS for the whole bring-up, shared by every wait inside it, so a bring-up where everything
      times out still finishes in time to SAY so.
    - AND THE ONE THAT COST THE MOST: `MAX_INITIAL_OFFERS` WAS FOUR AND THIS CONTROLLER NOW PUBLISHES
      FIVE. `common::online` returned `false`, every caller in the tree ignores that return, and the
      driver went on serving a machine where it had never reported READY - which reads exactly like a
      driver that enumerated its whole bus and then hung. The bound was not a considered ceiling; it
      was the number the drivers of the day needed, and a controller is precisely the kind of driver
      that grows publications because each class module it carries is one. It is eight now, and the
      refusal at the bound PRINTS, which is the half that actually cost the time.

  THE PROBE STAYS IN THE TREE beside the transport. It binds nothing, it costs one device and one
  boot, and it is how the next machine's shape - streams or no streams - is read in one run rather
  than in one implementation.

  **AND THE TWO OPEN POINTS ARE ONE PIECE OF WORK, WHICH THIS NOTE HAD AS TWO (2026-09-19).** Task
  management was attempted and MEASURED against the live device, and what it found is the reason
  both are still here:

  IN THIS TRANSPORT THE TAG IS THE STREAM. `TAG` is `STREAM_ID`, and the doorbell rings that stream
  for every pipe - so the status transfer a device answers on is posted on the stream matching the
  tag. A task-management request needs a tag OF ITS OWN (its header's tag is the request's, and the
  managed tag is the doomed command's, in a different field), and a device answering it answers on
  that tag's stream. This driver posts one stream, so the answer arrives where nothing is waiting:
  the live device says nothing at all - `the UAS device did not answer a task-management request`,
  measured, not predicted.

  SO A SECOND TAG IS A SECOND STREAM, AND A SECOND STREAM IS TAGGED CONCURRENCY. The stream context
  arrays are already grown for sixteen - the probe reads the endpoint companion descriptors and the
  controller sized them - so what is missing is per-stream rings and a completion routed by stream,
  which is exactly what several commands in flight needs. Whoever does one does the other.

  WHAT IS WRITTEN AND HELD BY FIXTURES, so the next attempt starts with the decisions rather than
  the bytes: `uas::task_management_iu` puts the REQUEST'S tag in the header and the MANAGED tag in
  its own field, both big-endian, because a driver that put the doomed command's tag in the header
  would ask the device to answer under a tag already outstanding; and `uas::task_done` is TWO codes
  and not one - `0x00` complete and `0x08` succeeded, which are not adjacent - because a driver
  checking for zero alone reads a successful abort as a failure and escalates to a unit reset that
  throws away commands that were fine.

  AND WHAT THE TIMEOUT PATH DOES TODAY IS SAID WHERE IT HAPPENS: clearing an endpoint halt tells the
  CONTROLLER to forget a transfer, and the DEVICE is still executing the command.

  **AND THE PER-STREAM MACHINERY IS IN, SO TASK MANAGEMENT IS ANSWERED (2026-09-19).** The note
  above said what was missing in one sentence - per-stream rings and a completion routed by stream -
  and that is what was written.

  THE ARRAY DRIVES TWO STREAMS AND NOT ONE. `Streams` holds a ring per driven stream at ids
  1..=DRIVEN_STREAMS instead of one ring at id 1, and a device advertising fewer entries than this
  driver drives is REFUSED rather than clamped, because a clamp hands back a transport with fewer
  tags than the caller was told it has. The high-speed shape, which has no streams at all, is still
  one plain ring - and an unbuilt ring answers `None` rather than address zero, which is the
  difference between "this transport has no second tag" and "this transport has a second tag at
  null".

  THE ROUTING IS THE TRB POINTER, BECAUSE AN EVENT CARRIES NO STREAM ID. An xHCI Transfer Event has
  the pointer, the length, the completion code, the endpoint and the slot in it, and no stream. Each
  stream's ring is its own page, so the page the completed TRB lives in IS which stream answered.
  `wait_for_status` checks that as well as the endpoint - and the mutation below is what that check
  is for.

  AND THE LIVE DEVICE ANSWERS NOW, WHICH IT DID NOT BEFORE: `task management answered` in the online
  line, from an ABORT TASK sent at bring-up naming a tag with nothing outstanding. That request is
  the one management function that is safe to send to a healthy device - SAM has it answer rather
  than act - so the path is exercised on EVERY boot instead of only when something has already gone
  wrong, which is the path nobody discovers is broken until then.

  MUTATION, AND IT KILLED MORE THAN THE ASSERTION. Posting the management answer on the COMMAND
  stream - the one-ring arrangement, exactly - gives `task management UNANSWERED`, the measured
  silence this note recorded, and then the suite DIES at 100 tests with the guest aborting inside
  the xHCI oracle. The misrouted answer stays posted on the command stream and the next command's
  status wait takes a response unit for its sense unit. So the stream check in `wait_for_status` is
  not belt-and-braces: without it the failure is not a lost answer, it is a block request answered
  out of the wrong information unit.

  AND THE TIMEOUT PATH TELLS THE DEVICE NOW, not only the controller. Clearing an endpoint halt makes
  the CONTROLLER forget a transfer while the DEVICE goes on executing the command, and a device still
  executing a read eventually writes into a buffer this driver has handed to somebody else. The
  status timeout issues ABORT TASK for the command's tag and escalates to LOGICAL UNIT RESET when
  that is refused - which is why `uas::task_done` is two codes and not one, because a driver reading
  a successful abort as a failure resets a unit whose other commands were fine.

  **AND SEVERAL DATA COMMANDS ARE IN FLIGHT NOW (2026-09-19), WHICH IS THE OTHER HALF.** The serving
  side was the remainder: a request that ran to completion before the next was read is a queue depth
  of one however many tags the transport has. A block request is now POSTED and left outstanding,
  and the loop that drains events answers the consumer that asked.

  THE CONCURRENCY IS ACROSS CONSUMERS AND THAT IS NOT A LIMITATION, IT IS THE WIRE.
  `driver_protocol::block` carries NO correlation id, so two replies on ONE channel must arrive in
  the order the requests were made - and a tagged transport answers in whatever order the device
  finishes. So tags are handed to requests from DIFFERENT consumers, each with at most one
  outstanding, and each reply goes back on the channel its request came from. Out-of-order
  completion is then invisible to everyone, because nobody sees more than one of them. A driver that
  ignored this would answer a consumer's read with another consumer's sector.

  ONE DATA PAGE AND ONE CONTROL REGION PER TAG, because "outstanding" is a statement about memory as
  well as about rings: two commands sharing a page is the second overwriting what the first is still
  moving, and both consumers would read the same bytes with neither request having failed.

  AND TWO THINGS HAD TO BE LEARNED FROM THE MACHINE RATHER THAN REASONED ABOUT.

  THE FIRST: NOTHING HERE IS WOKEN BY A COMPLETION. The service loop sleeps on its consumers and on
  the controller's interrupt, and this driver has always read transfer events by SPINNING over the
  event ring. The first asynchronous version therefore posted two commands and went to sleep holding
  both, and the consumer waited for ever - measured, and the silence read like a device fault rather
  than like a driver asleep. So completions are REAPED by polling, on the same budget a synchronous
  request gets, and everything else on the ring is still serviced on the way past: a ring drained by
  one consumer and discarded is a keyboard that stops working while a disk is busy.

  THE SECOND: FILLING HAS TO COME BEFORE REAPING, AND THE ORDER IS WHAT MAKES IT REAL. With the loop
  draining events before serving the next consumer, the first command finished before the second was
  posted and two tags were never outstanding together - the suite passed and proved nothing. The
  serve arm now takes every UAS consumer that is ALREADY ready while a tag is free, then reaps. It
  waits for nobody, and the cap bounds it.

  AND THE DRIVER SAYS SO ITSELF, ONCE: `2 UAS commands outstanding at once, each on its own tag`.
  Tagged concurrency is not something a driver can assert about itself in a comment - either two
  commands were in flight on this machine or they were not - and that line is the evidence, printed
  at the moment it first happened. It is absent from the run that passed without concurrency, which
  is how the false pass above was caught.

  THE ORACLE MINTS A SECOND CONSUMER, the way DeviceManager mints one, sends BOTH reads before
  reading either reply, and requires each to come back with the written sector FROM A BUFFER OF ITS
  OWN. Sending one and waiting for it would pass against a driver that never had two tags
  outstanding in its life.

  MUTATIONS, TWO, AND BOTH KILL THE GUEST RATHER THAN AN ASSERTION. Handing every request tag zero -
  the one-tag arrangement - fails at 100 tests with the second request overwriting the first's
  pending state and its data page, so the first consumer is never answered. Posting a
  task-management answer on the COMMAND stream gives `task management UNANSWERED` and then the same
  abort, because the misrouted answer stays posted and the next command's status wait takes a
  response unit for its sense unit. Neither failure is a lost answer: both are a block request
  answered out of the wrong information unit, which is what the stream check in `wait_for_status`
  and the per-tag buffers are for.

  AND THE PORTS WERE MEASURED AGAIN, WHICH THE TRANSPORT HAD MADE NECESSARY (2026-09-19). The run of
  2026-09-18 measured a transport that posted one command under one tag on one stream, and none of
  that is what ships - so it said nothing about this driver. `drivers,pci,slow,usb` passes on aarch64
  with 100 tests in 1513 s and on riscv64 with 102 in 1822 s, and both ports print the same two
  lines as x86_64: `2 UAS commands outstanding at once, each on its own tag` and `task management
  answered`. NOTHING IN THIS ITEM IS OPEN NOW.

  AND THE THREE-TARGET RUN HAPPENED (2026-09-18): the driver suites pass on all three ports, so
  "x86_64 only" no longer applies to this driver either.
- [ ] **USB CDC-ECM and CDC-NCM** network classes: share descriptor/control plumbing but
  keep their frame formats separate; validate MAC/MTU/filter descriptors, bound NCM
  NTB/datagram tables and malformed offsets, and feed the same capability-scoped frame
  transport NetworkService already consumes from `virtio-net`. Cover link transitions,
  zero-length/short transfers, disconnect and backpressure without a second network
  stack inside the driver.

  ITS WRITTEN PLAN (2026-09-17), which the rule above requires before any code.

    REQUIRES   the xHCI controller and its class-module execution model, which is what a USB class
                 driver IS in this system - a module inside the controller's process, holding no
                 claim and reaching the bus only through it; `drivers::descriptor` for the walk;
                 `drivers::usb_class` for the per-class budget; the frame wire `virtio-net`
                 publishes and the `net` provider kind, both of which already exist
    OWNS       `drivers::cdc` - the two network models' shared descriptor decisions and NCM's
                 transfer block - and `usb_net.rs`, the class module
    EXCLUDES   a second network stack of any kind. The driver moves frames and publishes a MAC and
                 an MTU; every decision about what those frames mean is NetworkService's, exactly as
                 it is for `virtio-net`
    GATE       `usb-net` on the controller's hub, and a round trip: an ARP request out of the guest
                 and the host's answer back in
    SIZE       one module and one class module, no new kernel mechanism and no new contract

  CLASSIFICATION: a class MODULE and not a driver, so the per-driver registry entry, claim and
  transactional bind do not apply to it - the controller holds those. What does apply is the class
  budget: an adapter is charged when it is admitted and the charge goes back when it detaches.

  THE TWO MODELS SHARE THE PLUMBING AND NOT THE FRAMING, which is why they are one module and not
  two. ECM and NCM are the same two interfaces, the same union and Ethernet functional descriptors
  and the same control requests; then ECM puts ONE frame in a transfer and NCM packs SEVERAL into a
  block with a table of offsets. Running them together would mean a module told which it was in
  every function.

  **WHERE IT STANDS (2026-09-17): ECM IS UP ON x86_64 AND ITS ORACLE IS A ROUND TRIP OFF THE HOST.
  NCM IS HOST TESTS AND NO BINDING PATH, AND THAT IS STATED RATHER THAN IMPLIED.**
  `driver.xhci: online (00:05.0, 5 device(s)) (keyboard) (pointer) (storage) (network)`. The adapter
  is on the controller's hub beside the keyboard, the tablet and the stick; it publishes `net` - the
  same kind and the same eleven-byte MAC-and-MTU handshake `virtio-net` publishes - so
  NetworkService consumes a USB NIC without knowing it is one.

  THE ORACLE IS AN ARP REQUEST AND THE HOST'S REPLY. The test mints a connection the way
  DeviceManager does, reads the MAC out of the handshake, sends an ARP request for the gateway, and
  requires the answer to come back. That crosses the bulk OUT endpoint, QEMU's user-mode stack and a
  standing bulk IN completion; reaching READY proves none of it.

  THE TWO REQUESTS A CDC DRIVER FORGETS, both of which leave a link that is up and silent - which is
  indistinguishable from a cable that is not plugged in:
    - SET_INTERFACE to the alternate setting that has the endpoints. A CDC data interface's setting
      ZERO carries no endpoints at all, which is the specification's way of saying "not carrying
      traffic"; everything else can be correct and nothing ever arrives.
    - SET_ETHERNET_PACKET_FILTER. An ECM device comes up forwarding nothing.

  AND ONE DEFECT THAT IS NOT ABOUT CDC AT ALL, which is the part worth carrying to every class
  module this controller grows. A synchronous wait here - a block transfer, a control request, a HID
  recovery - drains the event ring looking for ONE completion and offers everything else to the HID
  path, which does not answer for a network endpoint. So a frame arriving during a disk read was
  dropped AND its standing receive transfer was never re-posted, which stops the link for good
  rather than losing one packet. It is the same shape as the port change the controller already
  keeps in `PortSignal` and it is kept the same way. One slot is enough by construction: the class
  budget is one transfer in flight, and nothing re-posts until the stashed one is drained.

  WHY NCM HAS NO BINDING PATH, AND IT IS A HARNESS FACT RATHER THAN AN OMISSION. There is no NCM
  device model in this harness - `usb-net` is ECM - and a binding path nothing executes is a claim
  rather than a driver. Its framing IS implemented and host-tested against a device that lies: a
  block claiming more than arrived, a table pointer inside the header or past the block, a datagram
  running past what was sent, a pointer chain that names itself, and the CRC variant, which is a
  different format rather than a dialect of this one and is refused by name. The module refuses an
  NCM adapter out loud rather than leaving it addressed and silent.

  AND THE ONE THING THAT COST A ROUND TRIP WAS NOT A CDC DECISION AT ALL: THE DEVICE HAS TWO
  CONFIGURATIONS AND THE FIRST IS NOT THIS ONE. Every other walk in this tree reads configuration
  index zero and stops, because every other device here offers exactly one. An Ethernet adapter is
  the ordinary exception rather than an exotic one: the common ones publish RNDIS as configuration
  one and CDC-ECM as configuration two, so a driver reading index zero reads a configuration it does
  not speak and concludes the device is not an adapter. The diagnostic said so in one line - "no ECM
  or NCM interface" - which was true of the descriptor it had and false of the device, and that is
  the shape of the whole class of bug: a correct statement about the wrong bytes. The module now
  walks every configuration the device descriptor declares and takes the first that binds.
  AND THE SECOND ATTEMPT FAILED ON MY OWN TYPO IN THE FIX: the device-descriptor request was sent
  with a value of 1 rather than `1 << 8`, so the configuration count fell back to one and the loop
  read index zero again - with a new message, which is how it was told apart from the first failure
  rather than looking like the same one.

  AND THE MUTATION GATE REFUSED ONE OF THIS ITEM'S OWN TESTS, which is the second time in two items
  and is the return on running it rather than writing it. The test named "the endpoints are taken
  from the alternate setting that has them" asserted a property this module holds BY CONSTRUCTION -
  a candidate with no endpoints is never a candidate - so the planted defect changed nothing and the
  test passed. A test that cannot fail is not evidence. What IS a real choice is which of SEVERAL
  settings that all carry a pair is taken, and a device may publish them in any order: the module
  takes the lowest-numbered, the test builds a descriptor with alternate two before alternate one,
  and the mutation - take the first one seen - is caught.

  AND IT CHANGED WHAT A `no-iommu` BOOT HAS, WHICH IS A POLICY QUESTION AND NOT A BUG. `virtio_net`
  declares `iommu-required`, so on a boot with no translation it is refused and the machine had NO
  link at all - a state the boot test names in its own diagnostic. The USB adapter reaches the bus
  through the xHCI controller, which declares `trusted-untranslated` like every other controller
  here, so it IS admitted: the same boot now comes up with a link, over USB. Nothing about that is
  accidental and nothing about it is obviously right either. The reason a NIC declares
  `iommu-required` is that it receives attacker-chosen bytes by DMA, and that reason does not stop
  being true because the DMA is done by a controller with a USB cable behind it. WHAT SHOULD FOLLOW
  IS THE PROJECT OWNER'S: either a USB network class module is a reason for its CONTROLLER to require
  translation - which would refuse the keyboard on that boot too, and is probably wrong - or the
  class module itself carries a policy that the controller enforces per module, which nothing in this
  tree has a shape for yet. It is recorded here rather than decided in a driver, and the boot test's
  diagnostic line was corrected to say what is actually true rather than what used to be.

  THE BIND WINDOW IS 44 TICKS on x86_64 against the 200 the window allows, up from 40 without this
  module - the cost of one more device enumerated, its two extra descriptor reads and its four
  control requests. 60 tests pass across the boot, network, USB and driver tags with it in.

  **AND THE THREE-TARGET RUN FOUND THE DEFECT x86_64 COULD NOT (2026-09-17), WHICH IS THE WHOLE
  ARGUMENT FOR THE RUN.** This driver published its `net` provider UNCONDITIONALLY, the way the
  mass-storage channel has always been published - and that is right for block and wrong for a link.
  The block contract ANSWERS: a request with no disk behind it gets an error status, so an empty
  publication costs a consumer one refused request. A network link does not answer. Its contract
  begins with the DRIVER speaking - the MAC and the link MTU, without which NetworkService cannot
  build a stack - so a `net` provider with no adapter behind it is a service waiting for a message
  nobody will send.
  ON x86_64 THE ADAPTER IS ALWAYS THERE, so the publication was never empty and nothing showed. On
  aarch64 the machine's xHCI carries a keyboard, a tablet and a disk and no adapter at all: the boot
  wedged at seventeen of twenty-four services with NetworkService first in the missing list, and
  stayed there - not slow, STUCK, the same seventeen after six thousand passes.
  The two conditional providers - this one and the UAS target's block channel - are published only
  when their device is there, and a device plugged in later is published then, under the token this
  driver's offer list reserved for it. A zero handle in that list is an entry that keeps its token
  and is not sent, which is what makes the later publication the SAME publication rather than a
  second one.

  **AND THE NOTIFICATION ENDPOINT IS CONSUMED NOW (2026-09-19), WHICH IS THE ITEM'S "COVER LINK
  TRANSITIONS".** It is the one thing a frame pipe cannot say: from the data endpoints a cable
  nobody plugged in and a quiet network are the same silence - a receive transfer that never
  completes - and the control interface's interrupt endpoint is where an adapter distinguishes them.

  THE DESCRIPTOR PARSE FOUND IT WHERE IT LIVES, which is the CONTROL interface and not the data one:
  an interrupt IN endpoint there, where the data interface's interrupt endpoint would not be a frame
  pipe and is already skipped. The live adapter publishes one at address 0x81, which was MEASURED
  before anything was written to consume it.

  IT IS CLAIMED AS A THIRD PIPE, with endpoint type SEVEN - interrupt IN, where six is bulk IN and
  two is bulk OUT - its own ring and its own page, and the transfer is STANDING beside the receive
  one: an interrupt endpoint a driver configures and never reads from is one the device eventually
  cannot write to. The class budget charges for all three whether the adapter publishes one or not,
  because a budget that charged only for what a device happened to have would admit an adapter it
  could not afford and find out at the endpoint that was not there.

  A REPEAT IS NOT A TRANSITION. An adapter saying "connected" twice has not reconnected, and a
  driver reporting both has a log that reads like a flapping cable. And a notification this driver
  does not act on is answered AS ITSELF rather than as a link change - a driver that read every
  notification as one reports the link going down when the adapter said something else entirely.
  The decoder is in `drivers::cdc` with fixtures, including the one that matters most: a speed
  change whose header PROMISES eight bytes of rates in a transfer that carried none is refused,
  because reading them would read past what the controller wrote.

  LIVE EVIDENCE: `driver.xhci: the CDC adapter reports its link is up`, on every boot that binds one.

  AND TWO THINGS THE FIXTURE CANNOT CHECK, said rather than assumed. QEMU's xHCI model accepts the
  notification endpoint configured as BULK and delivers the notification anyway, so the endpoint
  type - which real hardware refuses - passes either way. And nothing ever reports the link going
  DOWN, so the transition this path exists for is exercised in one direction only. What IS pinned is
  the decode, by fixtures, and that the endpoint is found, claimed and read, by the live line above.

  AND THE SECOND ADAPTER IS NOT A BUDGET DECISION AFTER ALL, WHICH IS WORTH CORRECTING. This note
  said "one adapter, by budget". The budget is the SYMPTOM: `NetworkService` opens the FIRST `net`
  provider the catalogue publishes and gives the subscription back, because "its whole stack is
  built on the link it opened" - its own words. A second adapter would publish a provider nobody
  opens, which is the mechanism-with-no-consumer this file refuses. Two links is a NetworkService
  item, not a driver one.

  WHAT IS STILL OPEN AND IS NOT CLAIMED: one adapter, for the reason above.

  THE PORTS ARE IN (2026-09-19): the driver suites pass on
  aarch64 (100 tests, 1539 s) and riscv64 (102 tests, 2097 s) under `drivers,pci,slow`, and on both the controller reports
  `the CDC adapter publishes a notification endpoint at 81` and then `reports its link is up` - the
  notification endpoint this item owns, consumed on machines whose USB enumeration shares nothing
  with x86_64's.
- [x] **HID-over-I2C descriptor and protocol** (SHARED LIBRARY - closes on host tests, binds nothing):
  the standardized HID-I2C descriptor and register protocol over a bounded I2C bus contract and its
  host fake. No I2C controller driver exists and `ProviderKindName` has no I2C kind, so there is no
  bus to bind to and no QEMU model to bind against - which is what makes this half a library rather
  than a driver, and what lets it close.
  THE I2C CONTRACT IS OWNED BY THE FIRST IMPLEMENTED CONSUMER, not fixed to this item (corrected
  2026-08-30). This said "AND IT OWNS THE I2C CONTRACT" while the shared-contract rule three hundred
  lines below assigns it to whichever consumer is written first - and the earlier IPMI SSIF transport
  consumes the same bus class. Two statements, one contract, opposite owners.
  IMPLEMENTED 2026-09-13 as `hid-i2c`, and this item is the first implemented consumer, so the bus
  contract is stated here.

    REQUIRES   nothing that does not exist: a parser and a protocol over bytes, with no controller,
                 no claim and no device
    OWNS       `src/user/libs/driver/hid-i2c` - `I2cBus` and `SlaveAddress` (the bus contract),
                 `Descriptor`, `Command`, `Input`/`decode_input` and `Device`
    GATE       `host.hid-i2c`, 13 tests over a fake device made of bytes, registered in the
                 verification model and in the frozen release-required set in the same change

  THE BUS CONTRACT IS THREE OPERATIONS AND NO CONTROLLER VOCABULARY: a write, a read, and the
  WRITE-THEN-READ that every register access is. The third is not the first two in sequence, and the
  difference is the whole reason it is named: a repeated start with no stop in between is what stops
  another master, or the device's own register pointer, from acting in the gap. There is no clock
  rate, no bus number, no pin and no arbitration in it, so a controller item will IMPLEMENT this
  rather than having to be described by it - and the IPMI SSIF transport that consumes the same bus
  class takes it as it stands.
  AN ADDRESS IS A NEWTYPE BECAUSE THE RESERVED RANGES ARE NOT ADDRESSES. `0x00` to `0x07` and `0x78`
  to `0x7f` belong to the bus standard's general call and ten-bit addressing; a driver that addresses
  one is broadcasting rather than talking to a device, and an eighth bit shifted in by a caller that
  confused the address with the read/write byte is refused where an address enters rather than at
  each transaction.
  EVERY NUMBER THE DEVICE PUBLISHES IS CHECKED BEFORE IT IS A BUFFER LENGTH. The descriptor is refused
  when its own length field is not thirty, when its version is not `0x0100`, when its reserved bytes
  are not zero, when its report descriptor is empty or beyond this module's ceiling, when its maximum
  input length cannot hold the two-byte prefix that is counted INSIDE it, and when that maximum is
  larger than the bus will carry. The first two matter most and for the same reason: a device still in
  reset answers a register read with zeroes, which would otherwise decode as a descriptor promising
  nothing and be believed.
  AND A REPORT IS BELIEVED ONLY AS FAR AS IT ARRIVED. The declared length is checked in BOTH
  directions - longer than what the bus returned is the device asking for a read past the buffer;
  shorter than its own prefix, or longer than the device's own published maximum, is the device
  contradicting itself - and a zero length is the RESET INDICATION rather than an empty report, which
  is the only handshake this protocol has and the one a driver waits for.
  THE REPORT ID ESCAPE IS THE ONE PLACE THE ENCODING IS NOT A BIT FIELD: an id of fifteen or more
  travels after the opcode with `0x0f` in the nibble, so a driver that wrote `id & 0x0f` would address
  report 31 as report 15 and never notice. The tests assert the bytes that go out, not only the answer
  that comes back.
  IT DOES NOT PARSE A REPORT DESCRIPTOR: those bytes go unchanged to the generic HID parser
  `drivers::hid` already has, which is what keeps one report-descriptor implementation in this tree.
  WHAT REMAINS IS THE BINDING HALF, and it is the item below: it needs an I2C controller with a real
  fixture and the firmware-node device identity, neither of which has an owner yet.

- [ ] **HID-over-I2C binding** (BLOCKED TWICE: on an approved I2C controller item with a real
  fixture, AND on the unowned firmware-node device identity - this driver reaches its device over an
  ACPI/FDT-described I2C controller, which the group introduction already says and this bullet did
  not carry):
  reuse the generic HID report parser and expanded InputService
  vocabulary over the standardized HID-I2C descriptor/register protocol. Keep the HID
  transport independent of concrete I2C controllers; ACPI/FDT attachment supplies only
  the bus capability, address, IRQ/reset and descriptor location. Bound report lengths,
  power/reset transitions and interrupt storms; support touchpads/touchscreens without
  product-ID bindings.
- [ ] **USB Audio Class 1/2**: parse audio-control topology and streaming alternate
  settings, choose only explicitly supported PCM formats/rates/channels, schedule
  bounded isochronous transfers and adapt clock/feedback endpoints without unbounded
  drift. Present the same PCM transport contract as `virtio-snd` to AudioService;
  support playback and capture independently, propagate unplug/xrun, and reject DSP,
  MIDI and vendor extensions until their own vocabularies exist.

  **ITEM PLAN (2026-09-19), written before any code because the rule below says so.**

  CLASSIFICATION: `driver`, and it carries ONE piece of `kernel/bus infra` it does not merely
  consume: this xHCI driver has NO ISOCHRONOUS TRANSFER TYPE AT ALL. Measured, not assumed - the TRB
  constants it defines are Normal, Setup, Data, Status, Link and the command types; there is no
  Isoch TRB and no isochronous endpoint type in any of the four class modules. Every other USB class
  here rides bulk, interrupt or control. That piece is this item's, and it is the reason this item is
  not the size its one-paragraph description suggests.

  WHAT IT REQUIRES THAT ALREADY EXISTS: the xHCI transport's slot, ring, doorbell and event plumbing;
  `drivers::usb_class` for the per-class budget and its refusals; `drivers::descriptor`'s walk;
  `driver_protocol::audio`, which is the PCM contract this item's own text names - it was extracted
  on 2026-09-19 when `hda` turned out not to be speaking it, so this item inherits a written wire
  with an enumeration of its message shapes rather than a comment inside another driver.

  WHAT IT OWNS:
    1. ISOCHRONOUS TRANSFERS IN THIS xHCI DRIVER: the Isoch TRB, the isochronous endpoint types
       (1 OUT, 5 IN), the interval the endpoint descriptor states, and a transfer schedule that is
       BOUNDED - a stream of periods posted ahead is a ring this driver refills, not a queue it
       grows.
    2. THE UAC TOPOLOGY AND ITS ALTERNATE SETTINGS: the audio-control interface's header and its
       collection of streaming interfaces; for each, the alternate setting that carries an
       isochronous endpoint and the Type I format descriptor that says what it carries. ALTERNATE
       ZERO IS THE ZERO-BANDWIDTH ONE by specification, so a driver that never switched settings has
       an interface with no endpoints, which is the same trap the CDC data interface already has
       here and is already written down beside it.
    3. THE FORMAT CHOICE, AND IT IS A REFUSAL RATHER THAN A CONVERSION: this system's wire is
       48 kHz, stereo, signed 16-bit. A device that cannot be configured for exactly that is
       REFUSED and says so. Resampling belongs to AudioService if it ever belongs anywhere, and a
       driver that quietly halved a rate is a driver whose output is a semitone out with nothing
       reporting it.

  WHAT IT EXCLUDES, so the item cannot grow: CAPTURE, because the device model in this harness has
  no input (QEMU's `usb-audio` is an output device; `multi=on` makes it UAC2 with more channels, not
  a microphone) - and a capture path nothing can bind is what this file refuses beside NCM and
  CDC-ACM; FEEDBACK ENDPOINTS and clock adaptation beyond reading the rate, because an asynchronous
  sink needs a feedback endpoint the model does not implement; UAC2's clock-source topology beyond
  what is needed to reach a Type I format; DSP, MIDI and vendor extensions, which the item text
  already excludes.

  TARGET DEVICE MODEL: QEMU `-device usb-audio,audiodev=snd0` on the existing xHCI bus, which is UAC
  version 1 by default and one output streaming interface. Attached after the devices already there,
  for the reason the NVMe helper records.

  OBSERVABLE-EFFECT ORACLE, to this tree's convention and not "the driver reached Online": a period
  of the wire's own size is handed to the `audio` provider this driver publishes, it is ANSWERED
  `OK`, and the isochronous ring's position advances - the controller saying it consumed the buffer,
  which is the same claim the HDA oracle makes through the link position and the same reason: a
  driver that answered without moving anything answers identically.

  ITS MEASURABLE LIMITS: one audio device, by budget, with its endpoints, its ring bytes and its
  periods in flight charged at admission like every other class; a format refused rather than
  converted; a bounded number of periods queued ahead; and a bounded descriptor walk, because every
  number in a UAC topology is the device's.

  DEFINITION OF DONE FOR ITS CLASS: host tests over the pure decisions with deterministic malformed
  mutations before any transfer - the format descriptor parse, the alternate-setting choice, the
  isoch TRB's fields; the QEMU model proving the governed process, the DeviceManager binding and the
  destination-service contract; the bind window measured; registered in the verification model in
  the same change; and the QEMU device wired into the harness in the same change. Acceptance on
  aarch64 and riscv64 is a separate measurement on each and this plan does not claim it.

  **AND IT IS UP ON x86_64 (2026-09-19): THE SINK BINDS AND THE CONTROLLER TAKES A PERIOD.**
  `driver.xhci: audio sink bound - 2 channel(s), 16-bit, 192 byte packets`, and the controller's own
  report gains `(audio)` beside the four classes it already carried.

  THE ISOCHRONOUS TRANSFER TYPE IS IN, which was the item's real size. What it needed, each written
  where it is decided:
  - THE ISOCH TRB AND THE ISOCHRONOUS ENDPOINT TYPE. One and five are OUT and IN where six and two
    are the bulk pair every other class here uses, and a Normal TRB on an isochronous ring is a
    transfer the controller has no schedule for - which is the mutation this item's oracle catches.
  - `Start Isoch ASAP` RATHER THAN A FRAME ID. A frame id is a promise about WHEN, and a driver
    naming one has to know which frame the controller is on and how far ahead it may schedule. For
    a sink whose consumer paces it, the period arrives when it arrives.
  - ERROR COUNT ZERO, WHICH IS WHAT ISOCHRONOUS MEANS. The bulk endpoints write three - retry this
    many times - and an endpoint that retried would be delivering the last interval's audio in this
    one.
  - THE INTERVAL IS AN EXPONENT IN THE CONTEXT AND A COUNT IN THE DESCRIPTOR. A full-speed
    endpoint's `bInterval` is frames; the endpoint context wants log2 of MICROFRAMES, and one frame
    is eight - so a driver that wrote the descriptor's number gets a schedule eight times too fast.
  - ONE TRANSFER DESCRIPTOR PER SERVICE INTERVAL. An isochronous endpoint carries `max_packet` bytes
    each time the bus schedules it, so this wire's 2048-byte period is ELEVEN transfers against this
    device's 192, and the last one is short - which is not an error: the wire's period and the
    device's packet size divide evenly only by accident.

  AND THE UAC DECISIONS ARE IN `drivers::uac` WITH FIXTURES, each named for the mistake it plants:
  the sample rate is TWENTY-FOUR bits and little-endian, so a reader taking four bytes swallows the
  next rate's low byte; a rate-count of zero is a RANGE and not an empty list, so a driver reading it
  as a one-entry list takes the lower bound as the only rate; a format must match EXACTLY including
  the subframe size, because sixteen bits in a four-byte subframe carries the same samples in twice
  the bytes and a driver matching on resolution alone hands the device half a period; and only bits
  1:0 of an endpoint's attributes are the transfer type, which a driver comparing the whole byte
  reads as a different type on every device that sets the synchronisation bits - and this one does.

  THE FORMAT IS A REFUSAL AND NOT A CONVERSION. A device that cannot be configured for 48 kHz stereo
  16-bit is refused and says so; resampling belongs to a mixer if it belongs anywhere, and a driver
  that quietly took 44.1 for 48 plays everything a semitone out with nothing reporting it.

  THE ORACLE IS THE CONTROLLER'S OWN STATEMENT AND NOT THE DRIVER'S: the reply comes only after the
  transfer event for the period's last packet, so a sink that answered without moving anything could
  not answer at all. MUTATION: a Normal TRB in place of the Isoch one fails it with an empty reply
  against `OK`.

  AND ONE THING THE BIND-WINDOW TABLE SHOULD BE READ WITH. Adding a seventh device moved this
  controller's window, and re-running it moved it much further: 97, 164 and 181 ticks across three
  runs of the same build against an allowance of 200. What that number measures on this machine is
  mostly HOST LOAD, which is worth knowing before somebody reads a single reading as a regression.

  WHAT IS STILL OPEN: capture, and feedback endpoints and clock adaptation with it.

  AND "THE DEVICE MODEL HAS NO INPUT" IS CHECKED NOW RATHER THAN ASSUMED (2026-09-19).
  `-device usb-audio,help` on the pinned QEMU offers `attached`, `audiodev`, `buffer`, `debug`,
  `msos-desc`, `multi`, `pcap`, `port` and `serial`. `audiodev` is an output backend and `multi`
  adds output channels; there is no capture interface and no option that creates one. A feedback
  endpoint belongs to an asynchronous sink whose rate the host must follow, which is the same
  missing half.

  THE PORTS ARE IN (2026-09-19), AND THEY COST MORE THAN ANY OTHER ITEM HERE. The driver suites pass
  on aarch64 (100 tests, 1539 s) and riscv64 (102 tests, 2097 s) under `drivers,pci,slow`, each reporting `audio sink bound - 2
  channel(s), 16-bit, 192 byte packets`. `xhci_driver_enumerates_the_usb_bus` takes 87 s on aarch64
  and 824 s on riscv64 against a handful of seconds on x86_64: isochronous transfers are paced by the
  controller's microframes and an emulated controller pays for every one of them. That is the
  emulator's number rather than this driver's, and it is written here because the test sits silent
  for a quarter of an hour on riscv64 and reads exactly like a hang.
- [ ] **USB Bluetooth HCI transport**: BLOCKED: no owned destination service or IDL - the sandboxed
  Bluetooth service below is named and unowned. Then: bind the standard USB Bluetooth class endpoints,
  carry bounded HCI command/event/ACL/ISO packets over a capability-scoped controller
  transport and recover stalls/unplug. The driver stops at HCI: pairing, keys, L2CAP,
  profiles and radio policy belong to a separately sandboxed Bluetooth service, whose
  size/risk does not leak into xHCI or the transport process.
- [ ] **USB HID Power Device class**: BLOCKED: no owned destination service or IDL - the platform
  POWER-STATE service is named and unowned, and `system-power` carries reboot and power-off and
  nothing else, so this driver has no typed UPS or battery destination and no observable-effect path.
  Then: extend the common HID usage/value machinery for UPS, battery, load, voltage, runtime and
  alarm reports, publishing normalized state to that service. Capability-gate output switching and
  shutdown commands;
  bound collection/report complexity and preserve unknown vendor usages without
  interpreting them. Reuse generic HID transport instead of adding a UPS-specific USB
  parser.
- [ ] **USB CCID** smart-card class: BLOCKED: no owned destination service or IDL - the
  capability-scoped smart-card service is named and unowned. Then: parse class/slot capabilities, power and negotiate
  card parameters, exchange bounded APDUs with timeout/abort and surface insertion/
  removal through a capability-scoped smart-card service. Do not place PIN handling or
  authentication policy inside the transport driver; malformed lengths, sequence IDs
  and unsolicited slot changes get deterministic tests.
- [ ] **USB CDC-MBIM**: BLOCKED: no owned destination service or IDL - the typed modem service is
  named and unowned; NetworkService alone does not carry SIM, network or link state. Then: bind
  the standard mobile-broadband control/data interfaces,
  validate fragmented control messages and session/NDP tables, model SIM/network/link
  state through a typed modem service and pass IP datagrams to NetworkService under an
  explicit grant. Bound outstanding transactions and datagrams; no modem-management
  command or subscription identity becomes ambient authority.
- [ ] **USB Video Class (UVC)**: BLOCKED: no owned destination service or IDL - the camera
  vocabulary and its service do not exist, and this item explicitly refuses to tunnel video through
  DisplayService. Then: first add a bounded camera vocabulary for format/frame
  enumeration, negotiated stream parameters, timestamps and capability-scoped frame
  buffers; do not tunnel video through DisplayService. Then parse UVC control/streaming
  descriptors, negotiate uncompressed or already supported compressed formats, assemble
  payload headers into bounded frames over isochronous/bulk endpoints, and recover from
  frame loss, malformed FID/EOF sequences and unplug. No image decoder is duplicated in
  the driver; consumers use the P02M0093/P02M0096 codec leaves.
- [ ] **USB MIDI**: BLOCKED: no owned destination service or IDL - the shared bounded
  event/timestamp vocabulary it names does not exist and MIDI routing is explicitly outside
  AudioService. Then: support MIDI 1.0 event packets and the standardized USB MIDI 2.0/UMP
  path only after a shared bounded event/timestamp vocabulary exists. Preserve cable/
  group identity, ordering and timestamps, cap queues and SysEx size, recover from
  malformed packets/unplug and keep MIDI routing outside AudioService's PCM stream.
- [ ] **USB printer class**: BLOCKED: no owned destination service or IDL - the spool/stream service
  is named and unowned. Then: implement standard bulk printer transport, port status and
  reset behind a bounded spool/stream service; applications receive a job capability,
  not raw endpoints. Begin with already-rendered printer languages and explicit status;
  page description/rendering, discovery UI and vendor maintenance protocols live above
  or outside the driver. Cover backpressure, paper/error status and unplug mid-job.
- [ ] **USB Picture Transfer Protocol (PTP)**: BLOCKED: no owned destination service or IDL - the
  media/import service is named and unowned. Then: enumerate storage/object metadata and
  stream bounded object chunks through a media/import service rather than mounting a
  camera as an arbitrary block volume. Validate container length/transaction ID,
  paginate large object sets, preserve read-only operation first and capability-gate
  delete/capture. MTP/vendor extensions remain typed unsupported until separately
  specified.
- [ ] **USB Device Firmware Upgrade (DFU)**: BLOCKED: no owned destination service or IDL - the
  trusted UI/admin policy path it requires is named and unowned. Then: enumerate/download/upload/status state
  machines with transfer-size and manifestation handling, but expose mutating firmware
  operations only through a dedicated high-risk capability and explicit trusted UI/
  admin policy. Validate target identity and, where available, signed image metadata;
  survive disconnect and failed manifestation without claiming rollback the device
  cannot provide. Runtime mode is supported before DFU bootloader quirks.

## Where each item's specification and reference implementation come from

Written down because "is this implementable" was asked and the answer is not uniform, and because
two of the three obstacles have nothing to do with hardware.

THE READY-MADE DRIVER IS ALMOST ALWAYS LINUX AND ALMOST ALWAYS GPL-2.0, which cannot be copied into
this tree without changing what this tree is. Linux stays useful as a description of what real
hardware DOES - ordering, quirks, the register write that has to come second - and not as code. What
follows names references that can be read with a clear head - MOSTLY permissively licensed, and where
one is not the table says so IN THE ROW and the rule changes with it. (Corrected 2026-08-30: this
sentence said "permissively licensed" of the whole table, while three rows are LGPL and one is
GPL-2.0. Corrected again 2026-08-30: the three tables' third column was still HEADED "Permissive
reference" over those rows, and `docs/todo/TODO.md` still summarised the index as giving every item
a permissive reference - so a corrected prose policy sat above two places an implementer is more
likely to read. The column is now "Reference implementation and permitted use", every non-permissive
row carries its licence and OBSERVATIONAL ONLY, and the roadmap row says the same.)

Repository licensing applies to every row: LiberSystem remains under the Unlicense, and code under
another licence is not vendored or adapted. All listed implementations are observational references
for an independent implementation from public specifications, including permissively licensed ones.
The licence labels describe the references, not permission to import their source; the explicit
OBSERVATIONAL ONLY labels on GPL/LGPL rows reinforce that same boundary.

- **`virtio-drivers`** (rust-vmm), MIT / Apache-2.0 - `no_std` Rust, the whole of group 1's shape.
- **FreeBSD / NetBSD / OpenBSD**, BSD-2/3 - the widest coverage of group 2 and much of group 3.
- **EDK2 / TianoCore**, BSD-2-Clause-Patent - NVMe, AHCI, all four USB controllers, USB mass storage
  and HID, SD/MMC, TPM2, ACPI table parsing. Firmware-shaped, so often the smallest correct example.
- **Redox OS**, MIT - NVMe, AHCI, xHCI, virtio, HDA, in Rust.

AND FOUR ITEMS ARE BLOCKED ON SOMETHING THIS SECTION EXISTS TO MAKE VISIBLE: they need an AML
interpreter and an ACPI namespace, and this kernel has neither. It reads `APIC`, `SRAT` and `SLIT` -
static tables - and nothing else. `_BST`, `_BIF`, `_TMP` and the UCSI PPM interface are control
methods, so those items are not "waiting for a laptop", they are waiting for a bytecode interpreter
the size of the rest of this milestone. Marked **AML** below. The power button is NOT among them: on
a fixed-hardware platform it is a PM1a event named by the FADT, and WDAT and NFIT are static tables.

AND "WAITING FOR AN INTERPRETER NOBODY IS WRITING" IS NOT A STATE A CHECKBOX MAY BE IN (corrected
2026-08-30). This file said elsewhere that the interpreter is a separate milestone and not this one -
and there is no such milestone anywhere in the roadmap, so four checkboxes here had no event that
could ever close them while the Definition of Done demanded they close. Two consequences, both
recorded rather than left implicit:

- The AML interpreter and ACPI namespace are an ARCHITECTURAL PREREQUISITE with no milestone yet.
  It must be separately approved and numbered, with its own security scope - what a control method
  may touch, how an operation region is bounded, what an untrusted caller may never cause to run -
  and its own conformance evidence. Until it exists, the four AML items are NOT part of this
  roadmap's completion set; they are listed here as blocked candidates and are struck from any
  "every item is done" reading.
- The fixed-hardware power-button work is SPLIT OUT from the AML battery/thermal work, because it
  needs none of this: a PM1a event named by the FADT is a static-table path. It does NOT "close on
  its own" - corrected 2026-08-30 - because the parser, the event-delivery decision and the resource
  authority it needs are all missing; it is three items, and they are listed as three.

HID-over-I2C is the same shape and gets the same treatment. Its parser half may need an abstract I2C
contract and a host fake - which it OWNS ONLY IF IT IS THE FIRST IMPLEMENTED I2C CONSUMER, since IPMI
SSIF consumes the same bus class and is independently ordered - and it then defers end-to-end binding
because there is no I2C controller item and no QEMU model, so its hardware half has no event that
closes it either. It is therefore split: the PARSER
AND CONTRACT half is a SHARED LIBRARY item that closes on host tests and hostile fixtures, and the
BINDING half is blocked on an approved I2C controller item with a real fixture. The same split
applies to every other candidate whose only deliverable today is a parser: classify it as a shared
library and close it as one, rather than holding it against a bind gate it cannot reach.

### Group 1 - virtio

| Item | Specification | Reference implementation and permitted use | Note |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| virtio-iommu | VIRTIO (OASIS), free | `virtio-drivers` | already bound in this tree |
| virtio-rng | VIRTIO, free | `virtio-drivers` | smallest item in the milestone |
| virtio-serial multiport | VIRTIO, free | `virtio-drivers`, BSD `vtcon` | |
| virtio-vsock | VIRTIO, free | `virtio-drivers` socket device | |
| virtio-scsi | VIRTIO + SCSI SPC/SBC (T10, drafts free) | `virtio-drivers`, BSD `vtscsi` | needs the shared SCSI core; owned by whichever SCSI consumer is implemented first |
| virtio-crypto | VIRTIO, free | spec only; no permissive driver known | least mature virtio device |
| virtio-balloon / virtio-mem | VIRTIO, free | `virtio-drivers` (balloon) | its own prerequisites are the work |
| virtio-fs | VIRTIO + the FUSE protocol | `virtiofsd` (Rust, permissive) - the HOST half | large: FUSE is the real surface |

### Group 2 - generic and universal controllers

| Item | Specification | Reference implementation and permitted use | Note |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| NVMe | NVM Express, free and excellent | EDK2 `NvmExpressDxe`, BSD `nvme`, Redox | |
| AHCI/SATA | Intel AHCI 1.3.1, free | EDK2 ATA/AHCI, BSD `ahci`, Redox | |
| UEFI GOP / simple-framebuffer | UEFI spec, free | EDK2 | |
| 16550 UART | NS16550 datasheets, everywhere | EDK2 `SerialDxe` / `PL011SerialPortLib` shape, BSD `uart`, Redox - observational reference only | NOT trivial as a FIRST step: the legacy x86 path is blocked on three mechanisms this tree does not have, and the firmware-described MMIO alternative is blocked on device identity - see the item |
| ARM PL011 | ARM PrimeCell TRM, free | EDK2, BSD | blocked on firmware-node identity and the early-console handoff |
| SDHCI | SD Host Controller *Simplified* Spec, free | EDK2 `SdMmc`, BSD `sdhc` | full spec needs SDA membership; simplified is enough |
| EDID/DDC | **VESA E-EDID/E-DDC need membership** | permissive parsers exist; structure is public | the one place the authoritative text is not free |
| PCIe hotplug, AER, PME | **PCIe base needs PCI-SIG membership** | EDK2 PciBus | register layouts are documented publicly |
| TPM 2.0 TIS/CRB | TCG, free | EDK2 `Tcg2`, BSD `tpm` | transport is small; the command set is not |
| ACPI power button | ACPI (UEFI Forum), free | EDK2 | fixed-hardware PM1a event - **no AML**, but needs the FADT/GAS parser and the event-delivery decision, both separate items |
| ACPI battery, thermal | ACPI, free | — | **AML** |
| HDA | Intel HD Audio 1.0a, free | BSD `hdaudio`, Redox `ihdad` | large: the codec graph is the work |
| UCSI | Intel UCSI, free | — | **AML** on x86 |
| ACPI WDAT watchdog | ACPI, free | — | static table - no AML; QEMU emits no WDAT |
| ACPI Time and Alarm Device | ACPI, free | — | **AML** |
| EHCI | Intel EHCI, free | EDK2 `EhciDxe`, BSD `ehci` | |
| OHCI and UHCI | OHCI free; Intel UHCI free | EDK2, BSD `ohci`/`uhci` | |
| IPMI KCS, BT, SSIF | IPMI 2.0 (Intel), free | BSD `ipmi` | KCS and BT are small |
| ACPI NFIT / NVDIMM | ACPI, free | — | static table - no AML |

### Group 3 - USB device classes and the remainder

Every USB device class specification is a free download from USB-IF. What differs by an order of
magnitude is their SIZE, which is the column that matters here.

| Item | Specification | Reference implementation and permitted use | Note |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Generic USB HID expansion | USB HID + HID Usage Tables, free | BSD `uhid`/`ums`/`ukbd`, EDK2 | the parser exists here already |
| USB CDC-ACM | USB CDC (PSTN subclass), free | BSD `ucom`/`umodem` | small |
| USB Attached SCSI (UAS) | USB UAS + SCSI, free | BSD `umass`/UAS | large: streams and task management |
| USB CDC-ECM and CDC-NCM | USB CDC ECM/NCM, free | BSD `ucdce`/`urndis` | ECM small, NCM framing is the work |
| HID over I2C | Microsoft HID-over-I2C, freely published | Linux `i2c-hid` (GPL-2.0 - OBSERVATIONAL ONLY) | needs the I2C contract; owned by whichever I2C consumer is implemented first |
| USB Audio Class 1/2 | USB Audio 1.0 / 2.0, free | BSD `uaudio` | large, and UAC2 much larger than UAC1 |
| USB Bluetooth HCI transport | Bluetooth Core (HCI USB transport), free | BSD `ubt` | transport is small; the STACK above it is not, and is not this item |
| USB HID Power Device | USB HID Power Device Class, free | usage tables in the spec | small given a HID parser |
| USB CCID | USB CCID (USB-IF), free | pcsc-lite CCID driver (userspace, LGPL - OBSERVATIONAL ONLY) | moderate |
| USB CDC-MBIM | USB CDC MBIM, free | `libmbim` (userspace, LGPL - OBSERVATIONAL ONLY) | sits on NCM plus a control protocol |
| USB Video Class (UVC) | USB Video 1.1/1.5, free | `libuvc` (BSD-3), BSD `uvideo` | **largest item in the milestone** |
| USB MIDI | USB MIDI 1.0, free | BSD `umidi` | small |
| USB printer | USB Printer class, free | BSD `ulpt` | trivial |
| USB PTP | USB Still Image / PIMA 15740, free | `libgphoto2` (userspace, LGPL - OBSERVATIONAL ONLY) | moderate |
| USB DFU | USB DFU 1.1, free | spec is thirty pages | small |

### What this table changes about the plan

- **`virtio-rng` is the cheapest true start**, and it is the only one: free specification, a
  permissive reference, a QEMU model, no AML, and the one contract it creates - typed entropy
  ingestion - has exactly one consumer, which is `SYS_RANDOM_GET`.
- **Everything else drags a shared contract, and the second version of this list denied it as
  confidently as the first.** `NVMe` and `AHCI` came off because the first block driver written owns
  the canonical block wire. But `16550`, `PL011` and `virtio-serial` stayed on under the words "no
  shared contract to create on the way", while this same file says four hundred lines earlier that
  the serial contract has THREE CLAIMANTS AND NO DEFINITION. Whoever writes the first of them makes
  it - and multiport did, on 2026-09-15: the byte stream is the base, the line coding is a layer, and
  the shared cost the three of them were said not to have is `drivers::serial_port`, which exists.
- **AND `16550` IS NOT A CHEAP DRIVER BEHIND ONE SHARED DECISION EITHER** (corrected 2026-09-03).
  This paragraph called the three of them "cheap drivers behind one decision the three of them
  share", which carries the shared-contract cost and drops the other one - and the item and the
  architecture section both say what it is. The LEGACY x86 slice needs three mechanisms nothing in
  this tree has: a range-scoped REVOCABLE PORT-I/O authority, where the public driver resource
  vocabulary has no port-I/O object or syscall at all; legacy FIXED-IRQ routing and its revocation,
  which the MSI-X path does not provide; and an ATOMIC HANDOFF, because the x86 kernel programs COM1
  directly, owns IRQ4 and polls receive from the idle hook after userspace starts, with no transfer
  step. The alternative first slice - firmware-described MMIO UARTs - is blocked on the device
  identity prerequisite instead. So its cost is the shared serial decision PLUS one of those two
  blockers, and the table's Note column says so rather than `trivial`. `PL011` is blocked on the same
  identity prerequisite and its Note is already honest.
- **Cheap as a driver is not cheap as a first step**, which is the thing this list keeps getting
  wrong. The question is not how big the driver is; it is how many later items consume what writing
  it forces you to define.
- **The four AML items are not driver work and must not be picked up as if they were.** Whoever wants
  them writes an interpreter first, which is its own milestone and is not this one.
- **UVC, UAC2, HDA, UAS, virtio-fs and MBIM are month-scale**, and saying so here is cheaper than
  finding out in the middle of one.

## Order, gates and completion

- [ ] Land the three groups in this order as a PRIORITY, not as a gate. The first group is the
  virtio TRANSPORT this system already runs on plus the remaining virtio devices that share it; the
  second is what a real machine needs before anything can be installed on it; the third is breadth on
  a USB stack that already exists.
  (It said "the first group is what this system runs on today", and that is the premise the header
  and the group preamble already correct: SEVEN devices are shipped and running in the current
  profiles, and RNG, vsock, SCSI, crypto, balloon/mem and virtio-fs are phased backlog in the same
  group. Read as written, the sentence gave those six the priority of the running system on a
  rationale this file elsewhere disproves. What earns the group its place is the transport they all
  reuse - the work is smaller per device than anything in group two - not that any of them is in use
  today. Corrected 2026-09-01.) Within a group the order is by value
  rather than by cost, except that a cheap item unblocking an expensive one earns its place ahead of
  it. A later item never blocks shipping or testing an earlier finished one - AND AN EARLIER ITEM
  BLOCKS A LATER ONE ONLY WHERE IT IS AN ACTUAL PREREQUISITE. Development-only virtio-fs does not
  hold up NVMe and NFIT does not hold up CDC-ACM; reading the group order as permission would make
  every unfinished item in group 1 a gate on forty others for no technical reason.
- [x] **THE INDEX GETS A STATE OF ITS OWN** (kernel/bus infra - tooling; owned here because this file
  is the reason it is needed). LANDED (verified 2026-09-09): `check-milestone-index.sh` knows `[i]`
  - title-checked, never counted among the open rows, and its self-test refuses an unknown marker
  and an ordinary milestone parked as `[i]` - and `docs/todo/TODO.md` carries this index and
  `P02M0103` under it. Two rounds have declared this row "never ticked and counted in no
  phase's completion" and nothing implements it: `docs/todo/TODO.md` carries it as `- [~]`, and
  `check-milestone-index.sh` treats `[~]` as an ordinary OPEN state - its own comment says so - while
  failing only the `[x]`-over-open-tasks direction. So a tool and a reader both still see an open
  Phase-2 milestone, which is the exact ambiguity the correction was meant to remove.

  The bounded change: one explicitly SUPPORTED marker for a non-completable reference row, taught to
  the index checker. It KEEPS the title check, which is the half of that gate worth having on a row
  nobody ever ticks, and it is excluded from any phase-completion reading. The checker's self-test
  gains a case proving it REFUSES an unknown marker rather than silently accepting one - because the
  current script accepts any character between the brackets, so introducing a new marker without
  teaching the checker would produce a row that passes by not being understood. `P02M0103` carries
  the same `[~]` for the same reason and takes the same marker in the same change.

  Until it lands, the claim in the roadmap row is a statement of intent and the row says so.

- [ ] **NO ITEM IS STARTED WITHOUT ITS OWN WRITTEN PLAN.** The rule below says what an item states;
  this says when. Before any code, the chosen item gets an executable item plan naming its
  classification from the list in the head, its exact prerequisites from the matrix in the head, the
  contracts and files it owns, what it explicitly excludes, its target device model or fixture, its
  observable-effect oracle, its measurable limits, and the Definition of Done for ITS CLASS. Writing
  every item's triple in advance is still declined and for the reason already given - inventing
  prerequisites for drivers nobody has started is how a roadmap acquires answers before it has
  questions - but starting an item without one is how two parallel items make incompatible decisions
  about a contract they share.

- [ ] Each item states three things and they fit on one line each: what it REQUIRES that already
  exists, what it OWNS that does not yet, and the one named integration gate that proves it. AND THAT
  GATE IS NAMED CONCRETELY OR IT IS NOT A GATE (added 2026-08-30): the exact host test ids, the QEMU
  model and its arguments or the physical fixture, the target and resource matrix, the OBSERVABLE
  effect in the destination service - this tree's component-oracle convention requires an observable
  effect and rejects "the driver reached `Online`" - the two-instance topology where the standard
  permits one, the teardown assertion, and numeric performance and resource limits. The oracle is
  registered in the verification model in the same change, and any new QEMU device is wired into the
  harness in the same change. Generic prose describing what a gate would check is what this bullet
  replaced and must not come back. THREE
  SHARED CONTRACTS HAVE NO OWNER TODAY and will be discovered by whoever writes the driver that needs
  them: there is no common SCSI layer - SCSI lives inside `usb_storage.rs` - the block wire format is
  copied by hand into StorageService, virtio-blk and USB storage, and the provider vocabulary is a
  closed set of eight kinds in `system-manifest`, which the first item needing a ninth extends.
  P02M0164 catalogues more providers than the services consume; which service consumes which is the
  consuming item's, stated in its own bullet. On a
  target without an enforcing IOMMU every DMA driver in every group still follows P02M0153's explicit
  `iommu-required` versus audited trusted-untranslated policy, DECLARED THROUGH THE REGISTRY FIELD
  the DMA-policy item above owns rather than through a hard-coded kernel list: putting virtio-iommu
  first does not pretend it protects a native platform where that paravirtual provider is absent.

  AND TWO OF THOSE THREE CONTRACTS ARE ALREADY CONTRADICTED IN THIS FILE (corrected 2026-08-30),
  which is what "discovered by whoever writes the driver that needs them" costs when the roadmap
  names an owner anyway. Common SCSI is assigned to virtio-SCSI alone while UAS - independently
  ordered, and free to be written first - requires the same layer; and the I2C contract is assigned
  to HID-over-I2C while the earlier IPMI SSIF transport consumes the same bus class. Both are
  resolved by the rule this file already uses everywhere else and must use here too: the FIRST
  IMPLEMENTED CONSUMER owns the contract, whichever it turns out to be, and the others consume it. A
  fixed owner contradicted by a non-gating order is not an owner.

  THE EXISTING DESTINATIONS HAVE PARTIAL CATALOGUE MIGRATIONS. AudioService now subscribes to audio
  providers and NetworkService opens its first NIC from a catalogue snapshot; neither is still
  handed the old `SND`/`FRAMES` endpoint. Their device-side protocols and full multi-provider
  lifecycles remain the work below. New providers use P02M0164's catalogue and per-consumer `open`,
  with the destination contract extended only by the item that first needs the additional behavior.

  AND EVERY DESTINATION NEEDS THAT MIGRATION, NOT ONLY AUDIO AND NETWORK (corrected 2026-08-30). The
  previous version assigned it to "the first non-virtio audio or network slice" and left block,
  display, input, pointer and USB with no owner at all - while most of the drivers listed below must
  prove their effect through exactly those services. Merely publishing a provider satisfies no
  integration gate while the destination is still bound to the handle it was given at boot. So the
  rule is per destination, and it is a PREREQUISITE OF THE FIRST ITEM THAT NEEDS THAT DESTINATION
  rather than of one nominated slice:

THE OWNER IS THE FIRST ADDITIONAL PROVIDER OF THAT KIND, WHATEVER ITS TRANSPORT (corrected
2026-08-30). The rows below named "the first NON-VIRTIO slice" and then listed candidates, which
stranded every valid first consumer the list happened to omit: this same file says virtio-SCSI,
SDHCI and NFIT can each be the first new BLOCK publisher, and none of the three is in the old
StorageService row. The transport was never the point - the migration is needed the moment a
destination has a SECOND provider to choose between, and a virtio one qualifies.

AND A TRIGGER THAT HAS ALREADY FIRED IS NOT A TRIGGER (added 2026-09-02). These rows are written as
"the first item that publishes a second provider of that kind", which only works while the kind has
one. Where the shipping manifest ALREADY declares two - which it does for `block`, and does for
`console-bytes` in a development image - the migration is owed NOW, and the row names the item that
owns the second publisher instead of waiting for a third that cannot retroactively be the second.
Every row below was rechecked against the manifest on that date; the rule is written here so the
next reader checks rather than assumes.

    destination        who owns its subscribe + attach/detach/reconnect migration
    NetworkService     PARTIAL: it opens one NIC from a catalogue snapshot, then closes the
                       subscription; the stack stays attached to that connection. The first item
                       publishing a SECOND network provider - CDC-ECM/NCM/MBIM or another NIC -
                       owns persistent identity, link-state, detach/failover and reconnect
    AudioService       PARTIAL, AND THE ROW SAID DONE (corrected 2026-08-31). It reads a CATALOGUE
                       connection at bootstrap, subscribes to `audio` and opens a connection to the
                       first provider that appears - which is the DISCOVERY half, and it is what
                       proved the catalogue seam and carried P02M0164's connection-accounting repair.
                       It is not the lifecycle contract this table's own paragraph below requires,
                       and the previous version of this row claimed it was. What is actually there,
                       read out of the code rather than out of the intent:
                         - it opens only while its one slot is empty (`info.live && state.snd == 0`),
                           so a SECOND live provider is seen and ignored - there is no provider
                           identity to hold it under;
                         - on a withdrawal it cannot tell whether the event names the provider it is
                           using, says so in its own comment, and keeps the connection;
                         - a channel failure clears the slot and reopens nothing, so an alternative
                           whose publication was ignored is not picked up;
                         - the virtio-snd side is still an unversioned raw byte-length protocol.
                       So the first item that publishes a SECOND audio provider still owns the whole
                       of the common contract below - provider identity, the versioned device-side
                       PCM contract, attach, detach, failover and reconnect - and not only the
                       device-side half. Reaching a provider through the catalogue is a smaller thing
                       than consuming providers through it, and this row conflated them
    StorageService     ALREADY TRIGGERED, AND THEREFORE OWNED BY NAME (corrected 2026-09-02).
                       This row said "the first item that publishes a second block provider - NVMe,
                       AHCI, UAS, virtio-SCSI, SDHCI or NFIT". The trigger has already fired and
                       none of those six caused it: the shipping manifest declares `block` for BOTH
                       `virtio_blk` and `xhci`, so this machine has had two block providers for as
                       long as USB mass storage has worked. A trigger that has already passed cannot
                       be waited for - a future third provider is not the first second one - so the
                       migration is owed NOW and it is assigned to `xHCI maintenance` in section 0,
                       which is the item that owns the second publisher. That is the same shape the
                       ConsoleService row already uses for the same reason.
                       What it owes is the whole common contract below - provider identity, subscribe,
                       attach, detach, failover and reconnect - and one thing more that is specific
                       to block: the consumer is capped at four probe connections and assigns its
                       boot roles by lowest bus address, so a fifth matching volume is invisible and
                       a role is decided by arrival order rather than by format, origin and
                       `RootSelection`. That cap is P02M0164's own M2 and is recorded there; this row
                       names it because the migration cannot be called done while the destination it
                       migrates still cannot see the providers it is being given
    DisplayService     PARTIAL: it subscribes and reacquires after the current driver's connection
                       closes, and reports withdrawal delivery. Full provider identity, failover,
                       and the versioned device-side scanout contract belong to whichever is
                       implemented first: a second display provider here or P02M0103 a-wsi.
                       One migration is shared by both consumers
    InputService       PARTIAL: it opens one Input and one Pointer provider from snapshots and
                       closes both subscriptions. A second provider of either kind owns the live
                       identity, attach/detach, failover and reconnect migration
    ConsoleService     MIGRATED 2026-09-13 by `virtio-console maintenance` in section 0, which is
                       the item that owns it; the row is kept because what it required is what the
                       other rows still owe. It was the last consumer in this machine chosen by the
                       DRIVER'S NAME. What it has now is the whole of the common contract below: the
                       versioned device-side wire (`console-stream` in `liber:device@1`), provider
                       identity held by the three numbers the manager mints, subscribe, attach,
                       detach, failover and reconnect - and no private endpoint anywhere, which is
                       why `Catalogue::take_from`, the routing primitive that lifted a publication
                       out of the catalogue for a consumer that could not ask, has no caller left in
                       any image and is gone. A SECOND publisher of this kind is still owed a ROLE
                       the registry declares and the catalogue carries, because kind and version
                       alone cannot tell a system console from a development channel; virtio-serial
                       multiport is the candidate second one and owes it
    the USB stack      the first item that attaches a USB child at runtime

  Each completes the same three things: the versioned DEVICE-SIDE contract for that kind (PCM,
  frame-plus-link, block, scanout, event stream), service-side attach/detach/reconnect, and catalogue
  discovery for any connection still handed over privately. Reuse the catalogue discovery already
  implemented in the partial rows; do not build a second migration. An item whose destination has
  not been migrated may still be written - it publishes to the catalogue - but it cannot close its
  integration gate, and it says so rather than adding a second private endpoint.

  **AND THE ITEMS WHOSE DESTINATION DOES NOT EXIST ARE MARKED BLOCKED IN THEIR OWN BULLETS, NOT ONLY
  HERE** (2026-08-30). Bluetooth, smart-card (CCID), modem (MBIM), camera (UVC), printer, media
  import (PTP) and DFU/admin are named as destinations in the bullets below and HAVE NO OWNED SERVICE
  OR IDL MILESTONE AT ALL; so are UCSI's Type-C destination, WDAT's watchdog, IPMI's management
  destination, USB MIDI's event/timestamp destination, and the PLATFORM POWER-STATE service that
  `USB HID Power Device class` and `ACPI battery, AC and thermal classes` both publish to - the last
  one was missed when this invariant was first written, which is the failure it names happening to
  the paragraph that names it (added 2026-08-30). A previous round stated the rule here and
  left every one of those as an ordinary unchecked bullet, so an item plan could follow its bullet,
  publish successfully, and have no path to the observable effect its own gate requires - or expand
  into an unplanned service subsystem to invent one.

  Every such bullet carries **BLOCKED: no owned destination service or IDL** as the first words of
  its note, naming the destination it needs. A blocked item may be planned and may not be started,
  and the thing that unblocks it is an approved and numbered service/IDL milestone - not this item
  building one.
- [x] **THE HOST-TEST STRATEGY DOES NOT COMPILE TODAY, AND THAT IS A PREREQUISITE OF THIS SECTION**
  (added 2026-08-30). RESOLVED 2026-09-09 by the seam item in section 0, in the cfg/feature shape:
  `cargo test --manifest-path src/user/drivers/core/Cargo.toml` runs from the repository root, and
  the watched-fail mutations are recorded with the virtio-blk item. Every item below is required to have host tests over its pure parser layer, and
  `cargo test --manifest-path src/user/drivers/core/Cargo.toml --lib` fails before running anything:
  `error[E0152]: duplicate lang item in crate 'std': 'panic_impl'`, because the driver crate
  unconditionally depends on `rt` and `rt` declares an unconditional `#[panic_handler]`. So the
  mandatory strategy is not merely unwritten, it is unavailable.
  **IT IS OWNED BY THE PHASE-2 MAINTENANCE SUBSECTION BELOW, AS ITS FIRST ITEM** (2026-08-30). The
  previous version said "the first item that needs a host test owns the seam", which is not an owner:
  it is a race between whichever parallel item happens to arrive first, and two arriving together
  would build two seams. The maintenance subsection needs it before any of its own items can be
  gated, so it builds it, and every later item in this index consumes it.
  The shape: either a pure protocol/parser layer in its own crate that links no runtime, or a
  host-test feature on the driver crate that drops the runtime entry, panic handler and allocator
  hooks - plus a fake-controller seam the parser tests drive.

  **AND THE GATE FOLLOWS THE SHAPE, RATHER THAN NAMING ONE COMMAND BOTH MUST SATISFY** (corrected
  2026-08-30). A previous version permitted either design and then fixed success to
  `cargo test --manifest-path src/user/drivers/core/Cargo.toml --lib` - a command only the SECOND
  design can ever pass, because the driver crate's dependency on `rt` is unconditional and extracting
  parsers into another crate makes that crate testable without changing what the driver crate links.
  So one permitted implementation could not pass its own gate. The gate is therefore stated per
  design:

    extracted crate     `cargo test` over the EXTRACTED crate runs its parser suite, and the driver
                          crate is proved to contain no parser logic that the extraction was
                          supposed to move - otherwise the extraction is partial and the untested
                          half is the one that ships
    cfg/feature design  `cargo test --manifest-path src/user/drivers/core/Cargo.toml --lib` runs,
                          which is only achievable this way

  Either way a WATCHED-FAIL MUTATION proves a broken parser is caught by whichever suite the design
  produced. What is not acceptable is a gate that names a command the chosen design cannot run.

- [ ] Conformance and hostile-device gates for every family: pure descriptor/register/
  ring parsers get host tests and deterministic malformed mutations before MMIO/DMA;
  fake-controller tests cover completion ordering, timeout, reset, disconnect and
  resource cleanup; QEMU models or USB fixtures prove the real governed process,
  DeviceManager binding and destination service contract. Test at least two instances
  where the standard permits them. THE PROCESS HALF IS CONDITIONAL ON THE USB DECISION
  ABOVE, because as written it contradicted it: a class driver that stays inside the
  xHCI Domain proves the CONTROLLER's bind and its own per-class provider cleanup, and a
  split one proves a per-interface claim and bind. And what must not survive a driver's
  termination is a USABLE authority or provider - not every charged resource. A
  CONFIRMED teardown returns them; an unconfirmed one leaves them charged and
  quarantined, which is this tree's deliberate answer to frames a dead device may still
  be writing to, and a gate demanding otherwise would fail the safe behaviour.
- [ ] Integration and performance gates: fresh x86_64/aarch64/riscv64 userspace builds
  remain green; each architecture runs every controller QEMU can expose there, with
  explicit documented skips only for unavailable models. Storage drivers format/mount,
  read/write/flush and reopen through StorageService; network classes exchange frames
  through NetworkService; input classes reach InputService; audio drivers sustain
  bounded playback/capture through AudioService; UVC reaches a camera client. Record
